**Foreign relations of Israel** refers to diplomatic and trade relations between [Israel](9282173.md) and other countries around the world. Israel has diplomatic ties with 165 of the other 192 [UN member states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_member_states) as of 12 December 2020.[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] Israel is a member of the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN) and a number of other international organisations. Israel maintains full diplomatic relations with two of its Arab neighbours, [Egypt](8087628.md) and [Jordan](7515964.md), after signing peace treaties in [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty) and [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty) respectively. In 2020, Israel signed agreements establishing diplomatic relations with three [Arab League](52625.md) countries, [Bahrain](18933277.md), the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), and [Morocco](19291.md). As of 2021, Israel had formal diplomatic relations with 168 countries, while 28 UN member states have either never established, or have broken off diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-usres-2]

Israel's foreign relations are influenced primarily by Israel's strategic situation in the Middle East, the broader [Arab–Israeli conflict](7960202.md) and the [conflict with Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict) particularly over [Iran's nuclear program](721807.md), along with the [rejection](32359767.md) by regional states. Israel's foreign policy goals have therefore been to overcome diplomatic isolation and to achieve recognition and friendly relations with as many nations as possible, both in the Middle East region and further afield. Israel practices both open and secret diplomacy to further national goals, for example, [commercial trade](14690.md) and [science and technology cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel), importing raw materials, engaging in [military procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Procurement) as well as [exporting arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_industry_of_Israel) and military assistance, intelligence cooperation with its allies, and prisoner-of-war exchanges and other arrangements for hostage releases. It has also sought to foster increased [Jewish immigration](351513.md) to Israel and to protect vulnerable Jewish communities in the [Diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_diaspora), to offer [aid to developing countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_foreign_aid) and humanitarian assistance to countries facing large-scale disasters.[^cite_note-3]

Israel's close friendship with the United States has been a linchpin of its foreign policy since the establishment of the state. Until the [Iranian Revolution](347268.md) and the fall of the Pahlavi dynasty in 1979, Israel and Iran maintained close ties. [Iran](14653.md) was the second Muslim-majority country to recognize Israel as a sovereign nation after Turkey.[^cite_note-washingtoninstitute1-4][^cite_note-turkeyandisrael-5] In the mid-20th century, Israel ran extensive foreign aid and educational programs in Africa, sending experts in [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_research_in_Israel), [water management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Israel) and [health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_Israel).[^cite_note-6] China is also one of the few countries in the world to concurrently maintain warm relations with both Israel and the Muslim world at large.[^cite_note-middlekingdom-7]

During the 2000s, the [Israeli Ministry of Foreign Affairs](18133296.md) warned that the increasing influence of the [European Union](9317.md) would further isolate Israel in global affairs.[^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9] In the wake of a series of diplomatic rifts with Turkey and the rise of the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) in Egypt in 2011, Israel had increasingly unfriendly relations with those countries for a few years.[^cite_note-10] During roughly the same period, Israeli relations with many countries in Europe including Greece and [Cyprus](5593.md) in the context of the [Energy Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_Triangle) and in Asia, including China and India, were enhanced, largely on account of the growth of Israel's high-tech economy.[^cite_note-11] Israeli ties with Egypt have improved since the Muslim Brotherhood was removed from power there, while ties to Turkey have been uneven since their 2010 nadir.

## Membership in international organizations



&#x20;[Israeli flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_flag) at the United Nations building in New York

The first [international organization](18201224.md) which the Israeli government joined was the [International Wheat Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Wheat_Council), established as part of [Point Four Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_Four_Program) in early 1949. Israel has been a member of the United Nations since 11 May 1949.

Israel is a member of many UN agencies, including the Office of the [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md) (UNHCR) and the [Food and Agriculture Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization) (FAO). Israel also participates in other international organizations such as the [International Atomic Energy Agency](14984.md) (IAEA) and the [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO).[^cite_note-12] Israel left the [United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Educational,_Scientific_and_Cultural_Organization) (UNESCO) in a coordinated move with the US in 2019.[^cite_note-13]

Within the UNESCO, Israel was a member in many international programs and organizations. In the area of science, Israel was an active member of the [Man and the Biosphere Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_and_the_Biosphere_Programme) (MAB), the [Intergovernmental Oceanographic Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intergovernmental_Oceanographic_Commission) (IOC), the [International Hydrological Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Hydrological_Programme) (IHP), the [International Centre for Synchrotron-Light for Experimental Science Applications in the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Centre_for_Synchrotron-Light_for_Experimental_Science_Applications_in_the_Middle_East) (SESAME), and the [International Geoscience Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Geoscience_Programme) (IGCP).

Other notable organizations Israel is an active member of include the [Education For All](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_For_All) movement, the [European Centre for Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO-CEPES) (CEPES), the [World Heritage Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Committee) (WHC), the [International Centre for the Study of the Preservation and Restoration of Cultural Property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Centre_for_the_Study_of_the_Preservation_and_Restoration_of_Cultural_Property) (ICCROM), and the [International Council on Monuments and Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Council_on_Monuments_and_Sites) (ICOMOS).[^cite_note-14] Relations were carried out through the Israeli National Commission for UNESCO.[^cite_note-15]

Israel joined the [European Union](9317.md)'s [Framework Programmes for Research and Technological Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framework_Programmes_for_Research_and_Technological_Development) (FP) in 1994,[^cite_note-16] and is a member of the [European Organization for Nuclear Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Organization_for_Nuclear_Research) (CERN),[^cite_note-17] the [European Molecular Biology Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Molecular_Biology_Organization) (EMBO) and the [European Molecular Biology Laboratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Molecular_Biology_Laboratory) (EMBL).[^cite_note-18] It is also a member of the [Bank for International Settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_for_International_Settlement) (BIS) since 2003.[^cite_note-19]

On 10 May 2010, Israel was invited to join the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) (OECD).[^cite_note-invitation2010-20] Israel is a member of [NATO](21133.md)'s [Mediterranean Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Dialogue) forum.[^cite_note-21] In 2014 Israel joined the [Paris Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Club).[^cite_note-22]

After over 50 years of not being part of a regional grouping in the UN (effectively shut out of many internationals organizations), Israel joined the [Western European and Others group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_European_and_Others_Group) on a temporary basis in 2000. Israel joined on a permanent basis in 2014.

World map showing status of Israel's diplomatic relations in 2025

After the [establishment of the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_the_Establishment_of_the_State_of_Israel) in 1948, Israel was subjected to [Arab League boycotts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_and_political_boycotts_of_Israel#Arab_League_boycott) and attempts to diplomatically isolate the state. As of 2025, Israel has diplomatic ties with 165 out of the other 192 [member states](31969.md) of the United Nations, as well as with the [Holy See](13393.md), [Kosovo](17391.md), the [Cook Islands](7067.md), [Niue](21228.md) and [Somaliland](51425.md).[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] Some other countries recognize Israel as a state, but have no diplomatic relations. Several countries once had diplomatic relations with Israel, but have since broken or suspended them (Cuba and Venezuela in Latin America, Mauritania in the [Arab League](52625.md), Mali and Niger in non-Arab Africa, the Maldives in South Asia, and Iran until the [Islamic revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_revolution)). In addition, a number of countries (all members of the Arab League) that at one time had formal economic ties (primarily trade offices) with Israel, which fell short of full diplomatic relations, subsequently severed such ties (Morocco, Oman, Qatar and Tunisia; however, Morocco renewed ties and established diplomatic relations in 2020).

### No diplomatic relations

#### Member states of the United Nations

As of 2025, 30 United Nations member states do not maintain diplomatic relations with Israel (period of former relations marked in parentheses):

- Africa: [Algeria](358.md), [Comoros](5403.md), [Djibouti](17207794.md), [Libya](17633.md), [Mali](19127.md) (1960–1973), [Mauritania](18778516.md) (1999–2009),

  [^cite_note-ajties-180]

  &#x20;[Niger](21373.md) (1960–1973, 1996–2002), [Somalia](27358.md), [Tunisia](30188.md) (trade relations 1996–2000)

*(Algeria,[^cite_note-181]* *Libya,[^cite_note-182] and* *Somalia[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] do not recognise Israel)*

- Americas: [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize) (1985–2023),

  [^cite_note-186]

  [^cite_note-ilcut-24]

  [^cite_note-ajties-180]

Despite the lack of diplomatic relations, some of these countries accept [Israeli passports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_passport) and acknowledge other indications of Israeli sovereignty.

#### Other states



[Tzipi Livni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzipi_Livni) shaking hands with [Salam Fayyad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salam_Fayyad), 2008

Israel has no diplomatic relations with the following states or entities:

- [Taiwan](25734.md) (Republic of China), which recognizes Israel, but does not officially conduct relations (though there are unofficial relations) because of Israel's recognition of the People's Republic of China.

- [Other states with limited recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition): [Abkhazia](18933375.md), [Northern Cyprus](271409.md), [Sahrawi Arab Democratic Republic](38762388.md), [South Ossetia](318381.md), [Transnistria](376581.md) (Israel has not recognised the independence of any of these entities)

- [Sovereign Military Order of Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta)

### Limited relations

Comoros has no official diplomatic ties with Israel but the countries engage in mutual trade.[^cite_note-200]

Israeli citizens are admitted into North Korea with Israeli passports, but like other foreign visitors they are asked to deposit their passport with the local authorities and use specially issued local documents for tourists.[^cite_note-201]

## North Africa and Middle East

See also: [Arab League and the Arab–Israeli conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_League_and_the_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_conflict) and [Arab-Israeli relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab-Israeli_relations)



&#x20; Recognition of both Israel and [Palestinian State](241405.md)

&#x20; Recognition of Palestinian State only

&#x20; Recognition of Israel only

On 1 October 1994, the Persian Gulf states announced their support for a review of [the Arab boycott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_and_political_boycotts_of_Israel), abolishing the secondary and tertiary boycotts against Israel.[^cite_note-202]

### Algeria

Main article: [Algeria–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Algeria and Israel presently do not have diplomatic relations.

In the mid-1990s, while Israel and North African states slowly established diplomatic relations, Algeria remained one of the last countries to consider such a move. It was only when [Israeli prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_prime_minister) [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) met [Algerian President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_President) [Abdelaziz Bouteflika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika) at the [funeral of King Hassan II of Morocco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Hassan_II_of_Morocco) on 25 July 1999 that comments about rapprochement were made.

### Bahrain

Main article: [Bahrain–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

In 2011, amid [Arab spring uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%932012_Bahraini_uprising), Wikileaks cables published on *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* revealed some of the hidden relations between Bahraini and Israeli officials. In a meeting with the U.S. ambassador in February 2005, Bahrain's [king](1328181.md), [Hamad bin Isa Al Khalifa](392300.md), had bragged about having contact with Israel's national intelligence agency, [Mossad](27619007.md). He indicated that Bahrain was ready to develop relations in other fields as well. The king reportedly ordered officials to refrain from using terms such as "enemy" and "[Zionist entity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_entity)" to refer to Israel. However, he refused the idea of having trade relations, saying it was "too early" and would be postponed until the establishment of an independent Palestine state.[^cite_note-Haaretz_8.4.2011-203]

Both countries [agreed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain%E2%80%93Israel_normalization_agreement) to fully normalize relations in September 2020.[^cite_note-204]

### Egypt

Main article: [Egypt–Israel relations](21337265.md)



[Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin), [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter) and [Anwar Sadat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Sadat) at [Camp David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David)

Israel has had full diplomatic relations with Egypt since the signing of the [Egypt–Israel peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty) in 1979. In Israel, the 1978 [Camp David Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David_Accords) were supported by 85% of Israelis, according to a 2001 poll taken by the Jaffee Center for Strategic Studies, based in Israel.[^cite_note-205]

However, Egyptian public opinion of Israel is highly negative. According to an Egyptian Government 2006 poll of 1,000 Egyptians (taken at the time of the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War)), 92% of Egyptians view Israel as an enemy nation.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

Egypt has mediated several unofficial ceasefire understandings between Israel and [Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people), especially with the Hamas government in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).

### Iran

Main article: [Iran–Israel relations](3022965.md)



Iranian minister Reza Saffinia visiting the home of Israeli president [Chaim Weizmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaim_Weizmann), 1950

Relations between Israel and Iran have alternated from close political alliances between the two states, during the era of the [Pahlavi dynasty](338883.md), to open hostility following the rise to power of Ayatollah [Ruhollah Khomeini](26232.md). While Iran was the second Muslim-majority country to recognize Israel,[^cite_note-Zvi_Bar'el-208] the two states do not currently have diplomatic relations with each other, due to Iran's withdrawal of its recognition of Israel. The post-1979 Iranian authorities avoid referring to Israel by its name, and instead use the terms "the Zionist regime" or "occupied Palestine". [Iranian passports](14185983.md) bear an inscription that says, "The bearer of this passport is forbidden from traveling to occupied Palestine."[^cite_note-209]

Due to recent [rhetoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhetoric) between Iran and Israel, [development of nuclear technology](721807.md), and Iranian funding of the groups [Hamas](13913.md) and [Hezbollah](13919.md), tensions have risen dramatically between the State of Israel and the Islamic Republic of Iran,[^cite_note-210] especially after the [election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Iranian_presidential_election) of the hardline [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](7907317.md) in 2005. Comments made by Ahmadinejad, who has called the Holocaust "a myth" and for Israel to be "wiped off the map",[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] were perceived by Israel as threats of destruction.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221]

A large population of [Iranian Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_Jews) lives in Israel, among them former president of Israel [Moshe Katsav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Katsav), former chief of staff / Defense Minister [Shaul Mofaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaul_Mofaz), and former chief of staff [Dan Halutz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Halutz).

### Iraq

Main article: [Iraq–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

No diplomatic relations between Israel and Iraq ever existed, but the issue of establishing such relations was given some consideration. Following the American-British led [invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Iraq) in 2003, diplomats had been discussing the possibility of establishing diplomatic relations between Israel and Iraq. However, then-Iraqi Prime Minister [Iyad Allawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iyad_Allawi) said in 2004 that Iraq would not establish ties with Israel.[^cite_note-222]

#### Kurdistan Region

Main article: [Israel–Kurdistan Region relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Kurdistan_Region_relations)

In 2006, President of [Kurdistan Region](3949386.md) [Massoud Barzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massoud_Barzani) said: "It is not a crime to have relations with Israel. If Baghdad established diplomatic relations with Israel, we could open a consulate in Hewlêr (Kurdistan)." Israeli television broadcast photographs from the 1960s showing [Mustafa Barzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Barzani) embracing then Israeli defense minister [Moshe Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Dayan). In 2004, Israeli officials met with Kurdish political leaders. In 2006 the BBC reported that Israel was training Kurdish militias in Iraqi Kurdistan.[^cite_note-Israelis_'train_Kurdish_forces'-223] In April 2012, it was alleged that high-ranking Kurdish officials had collected the revenues of Iraqi oil that had been smuggled to Israel via the [Kurdistan Region](679693.md).[^cite_note-AAN-224]

### Jordan

Main article: [Israel–Jordan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_relations)



[King Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Hussein), U.S. President [Bill Clinton](3356.md) and [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin), Israel-Jordan peace treaty

Israel has full diplomatic relations in [peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace) with Jordan since the signing of the [Israel–Jordan Treaty of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_Treaty_of_Peace) in 1994, but relations remain somewhat tense. Over half of the Jordanian populations descends from Palestinian refugees, who overwhelmingly have negative views of Israel.

### Kuwait

Main article: [Israel–Kuwait relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Kuwait_relations)

Relations between Israel and Kuwait are generally hostile, mainly as part of the [Arab–Israeli conflict](7960202.md). In 2019 Kuwait's Deputy Foreign Minister Khaled Al-Jarallah said "Kuwait will be the last country to normalize relations with Israel".[^cite_note-225]

### Lebanon

Main article: [Israel–Lebanon relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_relations)

Israel and Lebanon have been overshadowed by war, including the [Syrian Occupation of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Occupation_of_Lebanon) (1976–2005). In August 2006, after the clash between Hezbollah and Israel, Lebanese Prime Minister [Fouad Siniora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Siniora) said that Lebanon would be the "last Arab country to make peace with Israel" because of the large number of civilians that were killed in the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-226]

Since the year 2000, and due to many wars with Hezbollah, Israel treats Lebanon as an "enemy state",[^cite_note-227] although it is considering the possibility of a [non-aggression pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-aggression_pact).

In 2008 a [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) survey found that negative views concerning Jews were most common in Lebanon, with 97% of Lebanese having unfavorable opinion of Jews.[^cite_note-228] In a 2011 survey again by the Pew Research Center, all of the Muslim-majority Middle Eastern countries polled held strongly negative views of Jews. In the questionnaire, only 3% of [Lebanese](17771.md) reported having a positive view of Jews.[^cite_note-229]

### Morocco

Main article: [Israel–Morocco relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Morocco_relations)

Moroccan expeditionary forces fought alongside a coalition of Arab countries in the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War) against Israel.[^cite_note-230] In 1986, [King Hassan II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_II) invited then Israeli Prime Minister [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) for talks, becoming the second Arab leader to host an Israeli leader after Anwar Sadat. Following the September 1993 signing of the [Israeli-Palestinian Declaration of Principles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), Morocco accelerated its economic ties and political contacts with Israel. In September 1994, Morocco and Israel announced the opening of bilateral liaison offices.[^cite_note-231] When the king died in 1999, then-prime minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) and the Moroccan-born foreign minister [David Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Levy_\(Israeli_politician\)) flew to Rabat for his funeral.[^cite_note-232] The foreign offices were closed in 2000 following sustained [Israeli-Palestinian violence](71717.md). In September 2016, Moroccan King Mohammed VI sent his personal adviser Andre Azulai, who is Jewish, to attend the state funeral of former Israeli Prime Minister and President [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres).[^cite_note-233]

Prior to the establishment of formal relations, Israeli tourism to Morocco was encouraged by the World Federation of Moroccan Jewry, a non-governmental private Jewish organization.[^cite_note-234]

On 10 December 2020, [Morocco agreed to establish diplomatic relations with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Morocco_normalization_agreement) in exchange for the United States supporting Morocco's claim on [Western Sahara](33220.md). On the same day, the United States agreed to the sale of sophisticated drones to Morocco.[^cite_note-235]

### Oman

Main article: [Israel–Oman relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Oman_relations)

In 1996, Oman and Israel agreed to exchange trade representation offices.[^cite_note-236]

### Qatar

Main article: [Israel–Qatar relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Qatar_relations)

Qatar and Israel do not currently have diplomatic relations, although they maintained economic relations between 1996 and 2000. Qatar is a major financial supporter of the Palestinian Sunni-Islamic fundamentalist group [Hamas](13913.md).

On 9 September, [Israel conducted airstrikes on Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrike_on_Hamas_leadership_in_Qatar) in an effort to eliminate the forthcoming Hamas leader. At that time, Hamas officials were engaged in discussions regarding a ceasefire.[^cite_note-237] Numerous global leaders condemned Israel for the assault. Both US President Donald Trump and Germany, who are allies of Israel, denounced the attack on Qatar, labeling it a breach of Qatar's sovereignty.[^cite_note-238]

### Saudi Arabia

Main article: [Israel–Saudi Arabia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_relations)

In 2005, Saudi Arabia announced the end of its ban on Israeli goods and services, mostly due to its application to the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), where one member country cannot have a total ban on another. However, as of August 2006, the Saudi boycott was not cancelled.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240]

In recent years, Saudi Arabia has changed its viewpoint concerning the validity of negotiating with Israel. It calls for Israel's withdrawal from territory occupied in June 1967 in order for peace with the Arab states; then-Crown Prince Abdullah extended a [multilateral peace proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Peace_Initiative) based on withdrawal in 2002. At that time, Israel did not respond to the offer. In 2007 Saudi Arabia again officially supported a resolution of the Arab–Israeli conflict which supported a full right of Palestinian refugees to move to Israel, which generated more official negative [reactions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Peace_Initiative#Reactions) from Israeli authorities.

### Syria

Main articles: [Israel–Syria relations](23533046.md) and [Iran–Israel proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict)

Syria's relations with Israel are very poor, due to the Israeli occupation of the [Golan Heights](7515865.md). [Ba'athist Syria](57134800.md) had close ties with the anti-Israel militant group [Hezbollah](13919.md) and with the [Islamic Republic of Iran](14653.md).

Since 2004, Syria has accepted the import of apples from farmers in the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), territory that it claims, through the [Quneitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quneitra) crossing. This was a result of the ongoing Israeli refusal to accept apples from Golan farmers (reportedly due to over-supply), which led to a plea by the farmers to the Syrian government to accept their produce before it became spoiled in order to prevent economic collapse. In 2010, some 10,000 tons of apples grown by [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze) farmers in the Golan Heights were sent to Syria.[^cite_note-241]

### Tunisia

Main article: [Israel–Tunisia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Tunisia_relations)

Tunisia participated in the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War), sending 1,000–2,000 troops to fight alongside a coalition of Arab countries against Israel.[^cite_note-242] The relations worsened further in the early 2000s when the [Second Intifada](71717.md) began, and on 22 October 2000, the state radio of Tunisia declared that [President Ben Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Ben_Ali) had decided to break all diplomatic ties with Israel following the "violence in the Palestinian-controlled territories".[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244]

### Turkey

Main article: [Israel–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations)



Turkish firefighting plane sent to aid Israel, 2010

Turkey was the first Muslim-majority nation to formally recognize the State of Israel.[^cite_note-Zvi_Bar'el-208]

In January 2009, the Turkish government's condemnation of the 2008–09 [Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%9309\)) severely strained relations between the two countries. Erdoğan harshly criticized Israel's conduct in Gaza at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md) conference in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos), Switzerland in early 2009.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

Relations between the two countries were further strained after the [2010 Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid).[^cite_note-247] On 2 September 2011, Turkey downgraded ties with Israel to second secretary level and suspended military co-operation between the countries.[^cite_note-248]

### United Arab Emirates

Main article: [Israel–United Arab Emirates relations](26287631.md)

Following the [assassination of Mahmoud Al-Mabhouh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Mahmoud_Al-Mabhouh) in the UAE in 2010, allegedly by Israel, the UAE announced that travelers suspected of being Israeli would not be admitted even if using a foreign passport.[^cite_note-249] Prior to 13 August 2020, UAE had not recognized Israel as a state, and the two countries lacked official diplomatic or economic relations. Prior to the announcement of the 2020 agreement, relations had improved to the extent that Israel opened an office in Abu Dhabi, albeit only as a mission to the [International Renewable Energy Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Renewable_Energy_Agency).[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251]

Israel and the United Arab Emirates announced a [deal to establish diplomatic relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_Arab_Emirates_peace_agreement) on 13 August 2020.[^cite_note-252] In July 2021, The United Arab Emirates officially opened an embassy in Israel, becoming only the third majority Arab nation to have full diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-253]

### Yemen

Main article: [Israel–Yemen relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Yemen_relations)

Yemen and Israel do not have diplomatic relations, and contacts between the two countries are very tense. People with an Israeli passport or any passport with an Israeli stamp cannot enter Yemen, and Yemen is defined as an "enemy state" by Israeli law.

## Sub-Saharan Africa

Israel has diplomatic relations with 42 of the 44 [Sub-Saharan African](27067.md) states that are not members of the [Arab League](52625.md), including a number of Muslim-majority states.

### Angola

Main article: [Angola–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Relations between Israel and [Angola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola%E2%80%93Israel_relations) are based on trade and foreign policy. In 2005, President [José Eduardo dos Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Eduardo_dos_Santos) visited Israel.[^cite_note-254] In March 2006, the trade volume between the two countries amounted to \$400 million. The Israeli ambassador to Angola is Avraham Benjamin.

### Botswana

Main article: [Botswana–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botswana%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

The two countries established relations in 1993. Neither has a formal consulate or embassy in the other country, but the two governments have cooperated on several development initiatives. Six Israeli-centered diamond companies have operations in Botswana.[^cite_note-255]

### Cameroon

Main article: [Israel-Cameroon relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Cameroon_relations)

[Henri Étoundi Essomba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_%C3%89toundi_Essomba), ambassador of Cameroon to Israel in 2012, serves as the dean of the diplomatic corps in Israel.[^cite_note-256]

Relations were cut off in the wake of the [Yom Kippur war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_war), but restored in 1986, and Cameroon and Israel now have many military and political ties, with Israel training and arming Cameroon's rapid reaction forces[^cite_note-257] and Cameroon voting against many anti-Israel resolutions at the UN.[^cite_note-258]

### Chad

Main article: [Chad–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

In November 2018, Chadian President [Idriss Déby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idriss_D%C3%A9by) paid a visit to Israel.[^cite_note-259] In January 2019, Prime Minister Netanyahu paid a visit to Chad and both nations re-established diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-260] In February 2023, Chadian President [Mahamat Deby](39073394.md) visited Israel and opened a Chad embassy in the Ramat Gan town in Israel.[^cite_note-261]

### Djibouti

Although Israel does not have diplomatic or official trade relations with Djibouti (a member of the Arab League), following a meeting between officials of both countries in September 1995, plans were then announced to open liaison offices in the respective countries' capitals, prior to the possible establishment of diplomatic relations between the two states.[^cite_note-262] However, such relations did not materialize.

### Eritrea

Main article: [Eritrea–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eritrea%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Eritrea developed relations with Israel shortly after gaining its independence in 1993, despite protests among Arab countries. Israeli-Eritrean relations are close. The president of Eritrea has visited Israel for medical treatment.[^cite_note-263] However, Eritrea condemned Israeli military action during the [2008–2009 Israel–Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%9309\)).[^cite_note-264] Israeli-Eritrean ties are complicated by Israel's close ties to Ethiopia.

### Eswatini

Israel established diplomatic relations with Eswatini in September 1968,[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] immediately following that country achieving independence from the United Kingdom. Eswatini was one of only three Sub-Saharan African states (the others being Lesotho and Malawi) that continued to maintain full diplomatic relations with Israel in the aftermath of the Yom Kippur War in 1973, and has never severed such ties.[^cite_note-MFA-Africa-265]

### Ethiopia

Main article: [Ethiopia–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopia%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

In Africa, [Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopia%E2%80%93Israel_relations) is Israel's main and closest ally in the continent, due to common political, religious and security interests.[^cite_note-266] However, relations were severed between the years 1973 and 1989. Many towns in Ethiopia are named after biblical Israel settlements, including Ethiopia's third largest city of Nazret ([Adama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adama)). Israel also provides expertise to Ethiopia on irrigation projects. Thousands of Ethiopian Jews ([Beta Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beta_Israel)) live in Israel. In 2012, Israel appointed a Beta Israeli of Ethiopian origin, Beylanesh Zevadia, as ambassador to Ethiopia.[^cite_note-267] In October 2020, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) visited Ethiopia.[^cite_note-268]

### Ghana

Main article: [Ghana–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghana%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Diplomatic relations with Ghana were established immediately following Ghanaian independence in 1957. Agreement on technical cooperation was concluded on 25 May 1962. On 24 May 1968, a trade agreement was concluded. A cultural cooperation agreement was concluded on 1 March 1973.

Relations were broken at the initiative of the government of Ghana on 28 October 1973, following the Yom Kippur war.[^cite_note-archives1973-269] Improvement in relations followed Israeli attempts to prevent Ghanaian support for the Palestinian Authority, which led to a state visit to Ghana by Israeli Minister of Foreign Affairs [Avigdor Liberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Liberman) in September 2009. During that visit, a bilateral agreement for agricultural cooperation was signed.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271] Diplomatic relations were restored in September 2011.[^cite_note-272]

### Guinea

Diplomatic relations between Israel and the Republic of Guinea were established in 1958, but were strained due to the Cold War, as the Israeli government supported US policy while the government of Guinea took a pro-Soviet line. These relations were broken on 5 June 1967 when war broke out between Israel and Egypt in the Six-Day War. After Israel's support to Guinea during [its fight against the Ebola virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebola_virus_epidemic_in_Guinea),[^cite_note-273] relations between the two states were restored on 20 July 2016.[^cite_note-274]

### Kenya

Main article: [Israel–Kenya relations](21610238.md)

Diplomatic relations were established in December 1963. Israel has an embassy in [Nairobi](21482.md) and Kenya has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md). In 2003, Kenya requested Israel's help in developing a national [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_energy) program.[^cite_note-275] In 2006, Israel sent an 80-person search-and-rescue team to Kenya to save people trapped in rubble when a multistory building collapsed.[^cite_note-276] Following the [2007 Kenyan presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Kenyan_presidential_election) Israel donated medicine to the [Moi Teaching and Referral Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moi_Teaching_and_Referral_Hospital) in [Eldoret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eldoret).[^cite_note-277] In November 2017, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) visited Kenya.[^cite_note-278]

### Lesotho

Lesotho was one of only three Sub-Saharan African states (the others being Eswatini and Malawi) that maintained full diplomatic relations with Israel in the aftermath of the Yom Kippur War in 1973.[^cite_note-MFA-Africa-265]

### Liberia

Main article: [Israel–Liberia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Liberia_relations)



Liberian President William Tubman with David Ben-Gurion in 1962

Liberia was one of the United Nations member states to vote in favor of establishing a Jewish state in Palestine in 1947.[^cite_note-279] Israel and Liberia established relations in the late 1950s. The administration of [William Tolbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Tolbert) severed ties with the Israeli government in 1973 in response to the Yom Kippur War, but they were re-established in 1983 by [Samuel Doe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Doe), who succeeded Tolbert via [coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Liberian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat).[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281]

### Madagascar

Both countries have a number of bilateral agreements in force.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-282]

### Malawi

Main article: [Israel–Malawi relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Malawi_relations)

Israel established diplomatic relations with Malawi in July 1964,[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] immediately following that country achieving independence from the United Kingdom. Malawi was one of only three Sub-Saharan African states (the others being Eswatini and Lesotho) that continued to maintain full diplomatic relations with Israel in the aftermath of the Yom Kippur War in 1973, and has never severed such ties.[^cite_note-MFA-Africa-265]

### Mauritania

Main article: [Israel–Mauritania relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Mauritania_relations)

Mauritania [declared war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_war) on Israel as a result of the 1967 [Six-Day War](29328.md),[^cite_note-Oz-283] following the [Arab League](52625.md)'s collective decision (although Mauritania was not formally admitted to the League until November 1973).[^cite_note-284] Mautritania did not reverse that declaration until at least 1991.[^cite_note-Oz-283]

Mauritania did not abide by moves to [recognise](1368578.md#The_legal_consequence_of_subsequent_events) Israel's right to exist in the same way as most other [Arab countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_countries) later did, staying true to the 1967 [Khartoum Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khartoum_Resolution) which, in reference to Israel, stated "no peace, no recognition, no negotiable."

Little public information exists, and an account of Israeli-Mauritanian relations must be inferred from a number of indirect known factors, such as: behind the scenes meetings between Mauritania and Israel in 1995 and 1996, said to be at the instigation of Mauritania's President [Ould Taya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ould_Taya);[^cite_note-Pazzanta-285] the establishment of unofficial "interest sections" in the respective Spanish embassies in 1996 in the two capital cities,[^cite_note-Pazzanta-285] leading to the exchange of diplomatic representatives in each other's countries [from 27 October 1999](9282173.md#Foreign_relations).[^cite_note-286] It is inferred from this evidence that Mauritania had reversed its declaration by then.

On 6 March 2009, the Israeli diplomatic delegation to Mauritania left after nine years of diplomatic ties, following a demand from the Mauritanian authorities to close the Israeli embassy in [Nouakchott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouakchott) within 48 hours.[^cite_note-287] The Mauritanian delegation to Israel left earlier without sending official notice to the Israeli [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](18133296.md).[^cite_note-288]

### Mauritius

Both countries have signed many bilateral agreements.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-282]

### Namibia

Main article: [Israel–Namibia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Namibia_relations)

### Niger

Main article: [Israel–Niger relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Niger_relations)

### Nigeria

Main article: [Israel–Nigeria relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Nigeria_relations)

Israel and Nigeria established diplomatic relations in 1960.[^cite_note-289] In 1973, Nigeria broke off contacts with Israel, but in May 1992, bilateral relations were restored.[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] Since April 1993, Israel has maintained an embassy in Abuja, and Nigeria has maintained an embassy in Tel Aviv.[^cite_note-mfa.gov.il-1] Many Nigerians go on religious pilgrimage to Israel.[^cite_note-290]

### Rwanda

Relations with Rwanda were established soon following independence of the African state. They were broken by the government of Rwanda on 8 October 1973, during the Yom Kippur war.[^cite_note-291]

In January 2019, transportation ministers of Israel and Rwanda announced plans to inaugurate regular [Air Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Rwanda) flights to Israel.[^cite_note-292] Then, in April 2019, Israel opened an embassy in [Kigali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali).[^cite_note-293]

In 2016, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) visited Rwanda. In 2017, Rwandan President [Paul Kagame](393593.md) visited Israel. In 2024, Israeli President [Isaac Hergoz](4645588.md) visited Rwanda to attend the 30th commemoration of the 1994 Genocide against the Tutsi.[^cite_note-294]

### Senegal

Relations with Senegal were established soon following independence of the African state. They were broken by the government of Senegal on 28 October 1973, following the Yom Kippur war.[^cite_note-archives1973-269] Israel and Senegal on 4 June 2017, announced the resumption of full diplomatic relations, which had been frozen after Senegal cosponsored a UN Security Council against Israeli settlements.[^cite_note-295] Israel returned its ambassador to Senegal, and Senegal backed Israel's candidacy for observer status at the African Union. In a trilateral partnership between Israel, Italy and Senegal, Israeli [drip irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drip_irrigation) systems are being installed to help farmers in 12 districts of rural Senegal.[^cite_note-296]

### Somaliland

Main article: [Israel–Somaliland relations](70738417.md)

On 26 December 2025, Israel became the first UN member state to formally recognize Somaliland's sovereignty and independence.

Israel plans to immediately expand its relations with Somaliland through extensive cooperation in the fields of agriculture, health, technology, and economy.[^cite_note-Somaliland-179][^cite_note-DaV6b-297][^cite_note-T3L29-298]

### South Africa

Main article: [Israel–South Africa relations](6699387.md)



[Danny Ayalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Ayalon) with South African Foreign Affairs DG Matjila

The [Union of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_South_Africa) was one of only four [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) nations to vote in favour of the [1947 UN partition resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947_UN_partition_resolution), which led to the establishment of the State of Israel. South Africa was one of the first states to recognize Israel; diplomatic relations between Israel and South Africa were established in 1948. After the [Sharpeville massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharpeville_massacre) of 1960, Israel became a harsh critic of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_under_apartheid), leading to a break in its relations with [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria). After 1967, Israel and South Africa became strategic partners again, and this lasted until 1987 when Israel joined the West in forcefully opposing apartheid.

Since the end of apartheid, South Africa's new government has been cold toward Israel and critical of Israel's policies towards Palestinians but has nevertheless ignored calls from pro-Palestinian South African groups to sever relations between the two countries.

As a result of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), the [African National Congress](2503.md) passed a motion calling on the government to close its embassy in Tel Aviv in November 2023.[^cite_note-299] The South African [governmental cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_South_Africa) also called on the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) to issue an arrest warrant for [Israeli Prime Minister](24898.md) [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and recalled the country's ambassador to Israel and withdrew all the country's diplomatic staff from Israel.[^cite_note-magome2023-300]

### South Sudan

Main article: [Israel–South Sudan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93South_Sudan_relations)

Israel recognized the Republic of [South Sudan](32350676.md) on 10 July 2011, and offered the new state economic help, following its declaration of independence the previous day from the mainly Arab Muslim north Sudan.[^cite_note-301] On 15 July 2011, South Sudan declared its intention to establish full diplomatic relations with Israel[^cite_note-SSudan-302] and, on 28 July 2011, it was announced that full diplomatic ties had been established between the two countries.[^cite_note-fullDepRecIsrForMin-303]

### Sudan

Main article: [Israel-Sudan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Sudan_relations)

On 23 October 2020, Sudan agreed to normalise relations with Israel in return for the [Trump Administration](52231341.md) removing Sudan from the United States' list of state sponsors of terrorism, unblocking economic aid and investment in Sudan.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305]

### Togo

In May 2009, Israel and Togo signed a "pact for cooperation in the economic, agricultural and educational fields" with each other.[^cite_note-306]

### Uganda

Main article: [Israel–Uganda relations](23545969.md)

In a joint Israeli-Ugandan project, a professor from the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem)'s Faculty of Agriculture conducted a survey of [Lake Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Victoria) with a Ugandan colleague from [Makerere University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makerere_University). They found that [Nile perch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nile_perch), introduced by the British sixty years ago, have decimated native fish populations, leading to malnutrition in the lakeside communities.[^cite_note-haaretz.com-307] She helped to set up artificial fish ponds to raise [carp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carp), which had disappeared from the local diet. The [United States Agency for International Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development) sponsored the digging of the ponds and sent villagers to [Kibbutz](106200.md) [HaMa'apil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaMa'apil) in [Emek Hefer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emek_Hefer) to learn spawning techniques. Graduates of the training program established carp farms.[^cite_note-haaretz.com-307]

### Zambia

Main article: [Israel–Zambia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Zambia_relations)

Israel and [Zambia](34415.md) maintained diplomatic relations from 1966 to 1973; they were re-established in 1991. Zambia opened its embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) in 2015, while Israel reopened its embassy in [Lusaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusaka) in 2025.[^cite_note-308]

### Zimbabwe

Main article: [Israel–Zimbabwe relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Zimbabwe_relations)

[Abel Muzorewa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abel_Muzorewa), the Prime Minister of [Zimbabwe Rhodesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zimbabwe_Rhodesia), visited Israel on 21 October 1983. He urged [Robert Mugabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mugabe) to establish diplomatic relations, saying his political policies hurt Zimbabwe's agriculture and technology industries. In March 2002 an Israeli company sold riot control vehicles to the Mugabe government, shortly before the nation's 2002 elections.[^cite_note-c-309]

## Asia

In addition to Turkey and Azerbaijan, Israel has diplomatic relations with 5 non-Arab Muslim states in Asia (Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan).

### Afghanistan

Main article: [Afghanistan–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghanistan%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Afghanistan, currently, has no relations with Israel. The Monarchy of [Afghanistan](737.md) did have spiritual relations with Israel, whether in secret or Tribal rules in place. The [Afghan Royal Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_Royal_Family) trace their origins to [King Saul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Saul) of Israel. Afghanistan was the only Muslim country that did not revoke citizenship when Jews, also known as descendants of [Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judah_\(biblical_person\)), migrated to Israel. Rabbi [Eliyahu Avichail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliyahu_Avichail?action=edit\&redlink=1) has published numerous books linking the [Afghans](2235.md) to the [Lost Tribes of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lost_Tribes_of_Israel).

### Bangladesh

Main article: [Bangladesh–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Both the Israeli government and general public supported the [Bangladesh Liberation War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War). After the independence of Bangladesh in 1971, Israel offered to recognise the new born country in as early as 1972 but Bangladesh immediately rejected the offer.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-Grover2000-311]

Bangladesh does not recognize Israel as legitimate and officially forbids its citizens to travel to Israel by putting 'Valid for travel to all countries except Israel' on Bangladeshi passports. Bangladesh supports a sovereign Palestinian state and an end to Israel's "illegal occupation of Palestine".[^cite_note-mofabd-312]

### Cambodia

Main article: [Cambodia–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodia%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Israel established diplomatic ties with [Cambodia](334751.md) in 1960. Ties were cut in 1975 due to the rise of the [Khmer Rouge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Rouge). The ties were restored in 1993. Israel has no embassy in Cambodia and Cambodia has no embassy in Israel. Instead, the Israeli embassy in [Bangkok](56615.md), [Thailand](30128.md), is accredited to Cambodia. Cambodian students study agriculture in Israel.

### China

Main article: [China–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



Israeli President [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin) meeting with Chinese Vice Premier Liu Landung on a visit to Israel

On 9 January 1950, the Israeli government extended recognition to the People's Republic of China, but diplomatic relations were not established until January 1992.

Since 1992, Israel and China have developed increasingly close strategic economic, military, cultural and technological links with each other.[^cite_note-israelnationalnews.com-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-Tepper-315][^cite_note-Katz-316] Israel maintains an embassy in [Beijing](18603746.md) and is planning to open a new consulate in [Chengdu](170375.md), its third in mainland China.[^cite_note-317] China is Israel's third largest trading partner globally and largest trading partner in East Asia.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] Trade volume increased from \$50 million in 1992 to over \$10 billion in 2013.[^cite_note-jerusalempost-320] Shared commonalities and similarities between the cultures and values of the two nations with ancient roots dating back thousands of years as well as convergence of interests have made the two countries natural partners.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-China_learns_Israeli_agri-tech-323][^cite_note-zionistcouncil.com.au-324][^cite_note-Katz-316][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-Gregory_Noddin_Poulin-326] In addition, China is one of the few countries in the world to concurrently maintain warm relations with Israel, [the Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine-China_relations), and the Muslim world at large.[^cite_note-middlekingdom-7]

China's status as a [potential world power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Century) has prompted Israel to maintain closer ties with China by integrating China's global influence with Israel's pragmatic economic management, political stability and its regional strategic importance in the Middle East.[^cite_note-China_learns_Israeli_agri-tech-323][^cite_note-auto-327][^cite_note-ynetnews1-328][^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-Walgrove-322] Beijing has appreciated Israel's political stability and diplomatic ingenuity and sees the Jewish state as one of the regional pillars for securing China's influence in the Middle East and the entire world.[^cite_note-ynetnews1-328]

China and Israel have developed close strategic military links with each other. Bilateral military relations have evolved from an initial Chinese policy of secret non-official ties to a close strategic partnership with the modern and militarily powerful Israel.[^cite_note-Katz-316] Israel and China began extensive military cooperation as early as the 1980s, even though no formal diplomatic relations existed.[^cite_note-G-329][^cite_note-F-330][^cite_note-B-331] Israel has provided China with military assistance, expertise and technology. According to a report from the [United States-China Economic and Security Review Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States-China_Economic_and_Security_Review_Commission), "Israel ranks second only to Russia as a weapons system provider to China and as a conduit for sophisticated cutting-edge military technology, followed by France and Germany." Israel was ready to sell China the [Phalcon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EL/M-2075), an Israeli airborne early-warning radar system ([AWACS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_Early_Warning_and_Control)), until the United States forced it to cancel the deal.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] Some estimate that Israel sold arms worth US\$4 billion to China in this period.[^cite_note-G-329][^cite_note-F-330] China has looked to Israel for the arms and military technology it cannot acquire from the United States and Russia.[^cite_note-auto-327] Israel is now China's second-largest foreign supplier of arms after Russia. China has purchased a wide array of military equipment and technology, including [communications satellites](45207.md).[^cite_note-G-329] China is a vital market for Israel's aerospace and defense industry.[^cite_note-G-329] Due to Israel's recognition of China, Israel has also limited its cooperation with Taiwan in order to foster closer ties with mainland China.[^cite_note-G-329]



[Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and [Matan Vilnai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matan_Vilnai) on a 2013 bilateral visit in China

Since the establishment of diplomatic relations, cultural exchange has been a major component of bilateral relations, as both sides recognize the importance of creating a strong foundation based on their ancient and rich histories.[^cite_note-334] China's receptive and friendly embrace of the Jewish people on its soil has been one of affectionate sympathy as the Chinese have developed a favorable view of Jews, admiring them for their contributions to humanity, their ability to survive, the sharing of Chinese values such as family, frugality, hard work and education, and being products of ancient civilizations have been an impetus towards the long and enduring friendship between the Chinese and Jewish peoples.[^cite_note-Gerstenfeld-335][^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-jweekly.com-321] Shared affinities and similar cultural commonalities has not only been an impetus for the close bonds between China and Israel but has also created a symbol of brotherhood between the two communities.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-336]

Though Israel established diplomatic relations with China in 1992, the ties between the Chinese and Jewish people remain centuries old in addition to Israel and China being products of ancient civilizations dating back thousands of years.[^cite_note-Gerstenfeld-335][^cite_note-337] The cultural similarities between the Chinese and Jewish civilizations with both nations originating thousands of years ago have drawn the two countries closer together making the two countries natural partners in the international community. Chinese Jews have been a major impetus in maintaining the strong nexus between the Chinese and Jewish civilizations.[^cite_note-Jpost.com-338][^cite_note-China_learns_Israeli_agri-tech-323][^cite_note-jweekly.com-321] Jews remain a small minority in China, but unlike many parts of the world, Jews have historically lived in China without any instances of [antisemitism](1078.md) from the Han majority populace contributing to mutual respect and admiration between the two peoples.[^cite_note-Gerstenfeld-335][^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-Revitalizing_the_silk_route-339][^cite_note-Olds-340] With the intrinsic affinity that the Chinese people feel for the Jews, relations between the two communities have been mutually close, harmonious and friendly, due to shared common cultural similarities between the two peoples resulted Jews enjoying equal rights and coexisting peacefully alongside the mainstream Han Chinese populace with instances of Jews assimilating into the Han Chinese community through intermarriage.[^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-Wald_2004_63-341][^cite_note-Asia_Times_Online-342][^cite_note-Lifestyle-343][^cite_note-BUCKLEY-344][^cite_note-345][^cite_note-Jpost.com-338][^cite_note-346][^cite_note-China_learns_Israeli_agri-tech-323] On a geopolitical scale, China has sought to maintain close relations with the Jewish state as Israel's regional importance, stability and influence in an otherwise volatile region has been an important asset for the expansion of China's influence in the Middle East and the entire world.[^cite_note-ynetnews1-328] Shared commonalities and similarities between the cultures and values of the two nations, ancient roots as well as convergence of interests have driven the two countries closer with respect to scientific, economic, diplomatic and cultural ties.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-347][^cite_note-Gregory_Noddin_Poulin-326]

China is one of Israel's closest economic allies in East Asia where both countries have placed considerable importance on maintaining a strategic and economic relationship.[^cite_note-israelnationalnews.com-313][^cite_note-348][^cite_note-Katz-316][^cite_note-349] The economic synergy has served the two respective countries greatly where Israel's global technological prowess combined with China's global economic influence, industrial manufacturing capabilities, and marketing expertise made cooperation between the two nations inevitable.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321] China including Hong Kong is Israel's second top export destination after the United States and has been the top market for Israeli exports in East Asia.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-kaipule1-350][^cite_note-middleeastnewsservice1-351] China is also Israel's third largest trading partner and export market after the United States and the European Union with China being Israel's largest export market in East Asia.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-352] Israel has sought China's enormous global influence on world affairs, large consumer market, broad industrial manufacturing scale, and burgeoning economic dynamism while China has sought Israel as a powerhouse of advanced technological wizardry and a wellspring of entrepreneurial acumen leveraging each other's complementary capabilities and resources.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321][^cite_note-Walgrove-322][^cite_note-353][^cite_note-middleeastnewsservice1-351][^cite_note-Al_Arabiya_Network-354] China has sought Israel's technology to increase its international economic competitiveness and risk management. With the advice and experience of Jewish entrepreneurs, innovators, and inventors from the high-technology sectors, China has utilized Israel's indispensable economic and technological contributions to foster its long-term economic development.[^cite_note-Wald_2004_19-355] China has also expressed desire for Israel's advanced technologies, particularly in fields related to agriculture, telecommunications, and defense.[^cite_note-jweekly.com-321] The scientific and technological advancements made by Israel have led many Chinese politicians to respect the country's ingenuity and creative inventiveness because they know of the contributions Israel has made to its economy. Israeli agricultural techniques, most notably [drip irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drip_irrigation), and solar energy technologies are seen as crucial to China's economic development.[^cite_note-China_learns_Israeli_agri-tech-323][^cite_note-Wald_2004_19-355][^cite_note-356] Throughout Israel's early economic history, many Israeli startup companies were acquired by major U.S. and Western European corporations. Since the 2010s, China and Israel enhanced bilateral economic ties with China connecting both Chinese and Israeli businessmen and investors to invest in each other's economies respectively. Chinese economic cooperation with Israel has seen substantial Chinese investment of more than US\$15 billion in the Israeli economy, spawning seed capital in Israeli startup companies, as well as the acquisition of Israeli companies by major Chinese corporations that incorporate Israel's know how to help the invigorate the development of the modern Chinese economy more efficiently. China now ranks second after the United States in collaboration with Israeli high-tech firms that are backed by Israel's Office of the Chief Scientist.[^cite_note-357] Major Chinese firms such as Fosun, ChemChina, Brightfood, Horizons Ventures and China Everbright have invested significant amounts of financial capital and resources across numerous Israeli industries.[^cite_note-atimes1-358] Chinese businessmen and major Chinese corporations hold Israel's business, economic and entrepreneurial acumen and technological expertise with high esteem and have sought to integrate Israel's know-how with China's marketing proficiency, industrial manufacturing capacity and aptitude for large consumer market scaling.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-atimes1-358][^cite_note-360]

### Hong Kong

Main article: [Israel-Hong Kong relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Hong_Kong_relations)

Israel and Hong Kong have full diplomatic ties as part of Israel's diplomatic ties with China. Israel has a consulate in the city, while Hong Kong is represented in Israel by the Chinese embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).

### India

Main article: [India–Israel relations](7199609.md)



[Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_India) and [Israeli flags](459004.md) in New Delhi during [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon)'s visit, September 2003

India established diplomatic relations with the State of Israel in 1992 and has since become Israel's strongest ally in Asia.[^cite_note-361] The two countries cooperate in anti-terrorist activities in the Middle East and Southern Asia. Israel is India's second largest arms provider and India is Israel's principal arms market, and the trade volume between the two countries has increased significantly in the past few years.[^cite_note-362] Co-operation has taken place in the space sector as well with [India launching Israeli satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TecSAR). India became the top source market for Israel from Asia in 2010 with 41,000 tourist arrivals in that year.[^cite_note-363]

Israel and India share intelligence on terrorist groups. They have developed close defense and security ties since establishing diplomatic relations in 1991. In 2009, Israel overtook Russia as India's biggest arms supplier; the U.S. even gave Israel approval to sell the [Phalcon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EL/M-2075) to India after earlier forcing Jerusalem to cancel a similar deal with China. India has bought more than \$5 billion worth of Israeli equipment since 2002. In addition, Israel is training Indian military units and discussing an arrangement to give Indian commandos instruction in counter-terrorist tactics and urban warfare.[^cite_note-364] In December 2008, Israel and India signed a memorandum to set up an Indo-Israel Legal Colloquium to facilitate discussions and exchange programs between judges and jurists of the two countries. According to an international opinion survey conducted in 2009 on behalf of the Israeli Foreign Ministry, India is the most pro-Israel country in the world.[^cite_note-From_India_with_love-365][^cite_note-366]

India-Israel relationship has been very close and warm under the premiership of [Narendra Modi](444222.md) since 2014. In 2017, he was the first ever Prime Minister of India to visit Israel.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] India was the largest arms customer of Israel in 2017. Defence relations between the two countries are longstanding.[^cite_note-369]

### Indonesia

Main article: [Indonesia–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



Indonesia purchased over 30 [Douglas A-4 Skyhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_A-4_Skyhawk) from Israel in the early 1980s, despite no recognition or diplomatic relations.

[^cite_note-370]

In 2012, Indonesia agreed to informally upgrade its relations with Israel and to open a consulate in [Ramallah](44132.md), headed by a diplomat with the rank of ambassador, who will also unofficially serve as his country's ambassador for contacts with Israel. The move, which was agreed upon after five years of sensitive deliberations, represents a de facto upgrading of relations between Israel and the world's most populous Muslim country. Indonesia has formally presented the move to open a West Bank consulate as a demonstration of its support for Palestinian independence. In fact, while the ambassador-ranked diplomat will be accredited to the Palestinian Authority/PLO, a significant portion of his work will be in dealings with Israel, and the office will fulfill substantial diplomatic duties as well as consular responsibilities. Israel and Indonesia quietly maintain trade, security and other relations. Israelis can get visas for Bali in Singapore, while many Indonesians come to Israel as pilgrims.[^cite_note-timesofisrael.com-371]

### Japan

Main article: [Israel–Japan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Japan_relations)



The [Museum of Japanese Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tikotin_Museum_of_Japanese_Art), Israel

On 15 May 1952, diplomatic relations were established with Japan at a [Legation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legation) level. However, the Japanese government refrained from appointing a [Minister Plenipotentiary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_Plenipotentiary) to Israel until 1955. Relations between the two states were distant at first, but after 1958, no break occurred, despite the [Arab oil embargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_oil_crisis) on several countries, including Japan.

### Kazakhstan

Main article: [Israel–Kazakhstan relations](21337528.md)

Both countries established diplomatic relations on 10 April 1992. The embassy of Israel in Kazakhstan opened in August 1992. The embassy of Kazakhstan in Israel opened in May 1996. Israel has an embassy in Astana and Kazakhstan maintains an embassy in Tel Aviv.

### Malaysia

Main article: [Israel–Malaysia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Malaysia_relations)

Israel and Malaysia do not maintain diplomatic relations and Malaysian passports do not allow entry into Israel. However, Malaysia and Israel has been engaged in trade relations; in 2011 Israel exported goods to Malaysia worth \$716.4 million and imported goods worth \$93.6 million.[^cite_note-372] A report compiled by the European Commission indicated that in 2010 Malaysia ranked 15th among Israel's major trade partners, accounting for 0.8% (€667.6 million) of Israel's trade in that year.[^cite_note-373]

### Maldives

Main article: [Israel–Maldives relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Maldives_relations)

The Maldives established diplomatic relations with Israel in 1965 and severed them in 1974.[^cite_note-maldives-196]

In 2009, under president [Mohamed Nasheed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Nasheed), the Maldives signed cooperation agreements with Israel on tourism, health, and education and culture.[^cite_note-374] In 2010, the Israeli government sent a team of eye doctors to treat patients and train local medical personnel in the Maldives.[^cite_note-maldives2011-375] However, the renewed relationship did not develop into full diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-maldives2011-375][^cite_note-376]

In July 2014, under president [Abdulla Yameen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulla_Yameen), the Maldives terminated the cooperation agreements with Israel and announced a boycott of Israeli products, as Israel launched a [military operation in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict). Foreign minister [Dunya Maumoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunya_Maumoon) also announced that the Maldives would fully support Palestinians at international forums such as the [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md) and offer them humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-377]

### Mongolia

In October 1991 the official relationship of [Mongolia](19271.md) and Israel began. [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon) visited in Mongolia in 2001, when he was prime minister in the [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) government. [Israelis](181296.md) wishing to enter Mongolia are exempt from arming a visa in advance. The Mongolian immigration authorities tend to provide visitors to the country a 30 days visa on their arrival. One can extend the visa fee and the extension is at the discretion of the authorities.

An academic delegation from the Hebrew University in Jerusalem visited the Mongolian National University in August 2010. During the visit, an agreement was signed for cooperation between universities. In 2012, the Israeli Deputy Foreign Minister [Danny Ayalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Ayalon) visited and signed an agreement with the Mongolian Minister of Education which included collaboration between universities and institutions of higher education, in which Mongolians will learn about Israel and the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) and Israel will learn about the heritage and [history of Mongolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Mongolia). It was also agreed on expanding ties between the two countries and embassies.

### Myanmar

Main article: [Israel–Myanmar relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Myanmar_relations)



[U Nu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_Nu) and [Moshe Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Dayan) in 1955

Myanmar (also known as Burma) was one of the first countries to recognize Israel and establish diplomatic relations with Israel. Myanmar has also become one of Israel's strongest allies in the region, in terms of both technical assistance and also the much debated and rumored military links. Premiers from both sides such as [U Nu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_Nu) and [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion) made state visits to each other's countries in the 1950s.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Myanmar sends agriculture researchers to Israel for training. This was further cemented in Israel's aid assistance during the [Cyclone Nargis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclone_Nargis) disaster of May 2008.

### Nepal

Main article: [Israel–Nepal relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Nepal_relations)



[Abba Eban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abba_Eban) and the King of Nepal at [Weizmann Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weizmann_Institute), 1958

[Israel–Nepal relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Nepal_relations), first established in 1960, are based on mutual security concerns.[^cite_note-VISIT-380] [Bishweshwar Prasad Koirala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishweshwar_Prasad_Koirala), [Prime Minister of Nepal](2888818.md) from 1959 to 1960, had a strongly pro-Israel foreign policy. [King Mahendra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahendra_of_Nepal) visited Israel in 1963 and maintained Koirala's special relationship. Until the 1990s Nepal was the only South Asian country to have diplomatic ties with Israel.[^cite_note-BEGINNING-381]

Nepal is one of the few Asian countries to have consistently supported Israel at international forums and at the UN. Nepal has maintained diplomatic relations and continues to support the right of Israel to exist within secure and internationally recognized boundaries. Nepal voted in favour of Security Council Resolutions 242 (1967) and 338 (1973), which upheld the right of all the states in the region to live in peace. Nepal has also welcomed every initiative from whatever quarter that seeks to resolve the Middle East problems like the Camp David Accord signed between Egypt and Israel in 1978 and the renewed peace process sponsored time to time by countries like the USA.

After the [2015 Nepal earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2015_Nepal_earthquake), Israel was one of the first to send aid to Nepal. Israel sent a delegation of 264 people for Search & Rescue missions, and over 95 tons of equipment, including a field hospital. It is estimated that about 12,000 Nepalese foreign workers are residing in Israel, most of whom are women working as caregivers.

### North Korea

Main article: [Israel–North Korea relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93North_Korea_relations)

North Korea does not recognise the state of Israel, denouncing it as an "imperialist satellite".[^cite_note-Haggard-192]

### Pakistan

Main article: [Israel–Pakistan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Pakistan_relations)



A Pakistani passport not valid for travel to Israel

Israel and Pakistan do not have diplomatic relations with each other, and [Pakistani passports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistani_passport) say 'This passport is valid for all countries of the World except Israel'. Israeli authorities told a traveller that Pakistanis could apply for a visa, and that they would issue a paper visa and put the entry and exit stamps on it.[^cite_note-382] Some Israeli leaders have expressed the belief that should diplomatic relations with Pakistan be established, then Pakistan could serve as a bridge between Israel and the [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world).[^cite_note-383] In 2008, the Israeli Foreign minister stated that "Israel considers Pakistan as its biggest strategic threat" in light of concern over the increasing [Taliban](30635.md) threat in Pakistan.[^cite_note-384]

### Philippines

Main article: [Israel–Philippines relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Philippines_relations)

On 29 November 1947, the Philippines (a U.S. territory until 1946) was the only Southeast Asian nation at the time to support the partition resolution at the United Nations General Assembly recommending a Jewish State in Palestine.[^cite_note-385] Israel and the Philippines established full diplomatic relationships in 1957. Embassies were opened in Tel Aviv and Manila in 1962. The two countries have enjoyed warm relations in all spheres. In 1997, the two countries signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) institutionalizing the bilateral political dialogue between the respective foreign ministries. The political dialog is accompanied by cooperation in trade and economy, culture, technical assistance, science, academic exchanges, tourism etc. There are between 37,155 and 50,000 Filipino workers in Israel as of 2004.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387]

### Singapore

Main article: [Israel–Singapore relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Singapore_relations)

Singapore and Israel have maintained strong and mutually beneficial bilateral relations that are characterized by a close and amiable bond and amicable rapport, where the two nations have consistently cultivated a closely-knit and cordial relationship ever since their respective establishments.
