This is in part due to both countries' perception of themselves as diminutive yet affluent sovereign states and regional economic powerhouses surrounded by neighboring Islamic countries characterized by their comparatively underdeveloped societies, political instabilities, and economic struggles that have remained hostile to their continued existence in which oftentimes they have an uneasy relationship with.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-MalayWorld-389] Following Singapore's sudden and unexpected [independence](36205345.md) after being unilaterally ejected from Malaysia in 1963, Singapore appealed to the international community for technical assistance and military aid. Israel responded by assisting the Southeast Asian city-state with the establishment of [Singapore's armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Armed_Forces).[^cite_note-390] Both countries formally established diplomatic relations in May 1969, although unofficial and discrete relations were already established several years prior, particularly with regards to military activities. Singapore and Israel are also known to share a special relationship where they both enjoy an extensive bilateral security partnership, with Israeli and Singaporean arms contractors such as [Israel Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Aerospace_Industries) and [ST Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ST_Engineering) collaborating and engaging in joint venture developments and facilitating a large level of arms trade between the two countries.[^cite_note-HaaretzMar20-391][^cite_note-Israel_Shows_Wares_in_Singapore-392] Over the years, Israel has continued to advise Singapore on an array of military affairs, ranging from night operations to aviation psychology. The defense and intelligence establishments of both countries conduct routine exchanges of information, and a small number of Israeli Defense Force officers serve in staff appointments within the Singapore [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Singapore\)) (MINDEF).[^cite_note-393] In 2012, it was reported that Singapore expressed interest in purchasing several [Iron Dome](18342427.md) defence system units and a deal took place in 2016.[^cite_note-394]

### South Korea

Main article: [Israel–South Korea relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93South_Korea_relations)

The Republic of Korea and the State of Israel established diplomatic relations on 10 April 1962.[^cite_note-ROK_Israel-395][^cite_note-Israel_ROK-396] Israel opened its embassy in [Seoul](19159283.md) in April 1968, which was closed by the Israeli government in 1978. The embassy was reopened in January 1992, with Korea opening its resident embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) in December 1993.[^cite_note-ROK_Israel-395]

On 23 August 2010, Korea Venture Investment Corp. (KVIC), a South Korean state-backed fund and investment management company, signed a memorandum of understanding with Israel's Vertex Venture Capital (VVC) to raise a US\$150 million fund, which will be used to finance joint ventures or the [merger and acquisition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merger_and_acquisition) of small and medium-size venture capital firms between the two countries.[^cite_note-397]

The two countries signed a free trade agreement in May 2021, making South Korea the first East Asian country to forge a free trade deal with Israel.[^cite_note-398]

### Sri Lanka

Main article: [Israel–Sri Lanka relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations)

Israel was a source of weapons and training for the [Sri Lanka Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Armed_Forces) during the war against [Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) terrorist organization and weapons sold to the country, including [IAI Kfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAI_Kfir) Fighter Jet, the [Saar 4 class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saar_4_class_missile_boat) [missile boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missile_boat), [Super Dvora Mk II-class patrol boat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Dvora_Mk_II-class_patrol_boat) and the [Gabriel missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_\(missile\)).[^cite_note-399] In May 2011, the Israeli Minister of Agriculture visited Sri Lanka with an [agro-business delegation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Israel) to promote cooperation between the two countries.[^cite_note-400]

### Thailand

Main article: [Israel–Thailand relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Thailand_relations)

Thailand and Israel have had full diplomatic relations since 23 June 1954. The Israeli embassy was opened in 1958 although the Thai embassy in Tel Aviv only opened in 1996. Since the beginning, both countries have enjoyed strong ties and beneficial bilateral cooperation in many fields, most notably in agriculture and education. Thousands of Thai academics have been sent to train in Israel while many Thai schools have been modeled after Israel's experience and know-how with aid from [Mashav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mashav). State visits by Thai royalty to Israel have been reciprocated by Israel's public figures. 100,000 Israeli tourists visit Thailand annually.[^cite_note-401] Thousands of skilled and unskilled Thai workers are employed in Israel and many Thai students study in Israel.

There is also a Thai-Israel Chamber of Commerce, Thai-Israel Friendship Foundation as well as a small community of Israelis living in Thailand.[^cite_note-402]

### Turkmenistan

In 2013, the Israel Foreign Ministry opened a new embassy in [Ashgabat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashgabat), Turkmenistan.[^cite_note-JP2009-403][^cite_note-404]

### Vietnam

Main article: [Israel–Vietnam relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Vietnam_relations)

Vietnam and Israel established diplomatic relations on 12 July 1993. Israel opened its resident embassy in [Hanoi](56667.md) in December 1993.[^cite_note-vietOver-405] The first Vietnamese ambassador to Israel presented his credentials on 8 July 2009.[^cite_note-newAmb-406] Since the establishment of diplomatic relations, the two countries have frequently conducted reciprocal visits at various levels, and have strengthened ties in such fields as business, education, culture, technological cooperation and agriculture. The visits arranged by the Israeli government included those of delegations comprising entrepreneurs and businessmen, academic groups, journalists, artists and musicians, legal workers, and so on.[^cite_note-israOver-407]

## Europe

See also: [Israel–European Union relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93European_Union_relations)

### Albania

Main article: [Albania–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Israel and Albania established diplomatic relations on 20 August 1991. Albania had previously recognized Israel as a state since 1949.[^cite_note-408] Albania has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and Israel also has an embassy in [Tirana](31075.md).

### Armenia

Main article: [Armenia–Israel relations](13073170.md)

Since independence, [Armenia](10918072.md) has received support from Israel and today remains one of its major trade partners. Both countries established diplomatic relations on 4 April 1992. Israel maintains a consulate in [Yerevan](34352.md), while Armenia has an embassy in Tel-Aviv and an honorary consulate in Jerusalem.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] Israel has recognized 10 Armenians as [Righteous Among the Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Righteous_Among_the_Nations) for risking their lives to save Jews during the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust).[^cite_note-411]

### Austria

Main article: [Austria–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Austria recognized Israel on 5 March 1949. Austria has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 3 honorary consulates (in [Eilat](66162.md), [Haifa](55606.md) and [Jerusalem](16043.md)).[^cite_note-412] Israel has an embassy in [Vienna](55866.md).[^cite_note-413] Both countries are full members of the [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean). The Austrian Foreign Ministry lists the bilateral treaties with Israel.[^cite_note-414]

### Azerbaijan

Main article: [Azerbaijan–Israel relations](11716339.md)

Azerbaijani–Israeli relations are good, and Israel has an embassy in Baku. In May 1999, the U.S.-Azerbaijan Council sponsored a seminar to discuss relations among Azeris, Jews, and Israel. In April 2000, an Israeli trade delegation visited Baku to discuss ways of strengthening bilateral economic relations.

The Azerbaijan–Israel Friendship Society facilitates and promotes bilateral diplomatic and business links. In October 2001, President Aliyev pledged to open an embassy in Israel and send his Foreign Minister to visit the country. Although neither has occurred, Azerbaijani–Israeli strategic cooperation continues to grow.

For many years, Azerbaijan has maintained high rates of immigration to Israel due to the economic and political situation in the country. In 2002, 475 Jews made [aliyah](351513.md) and 111 immigrated to the United States. The Azeri government gets regular updates from Israel regarding Azeri Jews in Israel, who are plagued by unemployment, crime, and other social issues as new immigrants in Israel.[^cite_note-415]

### Belarus

Main article: [Belarus–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Israel established relations with Belarus in 1992 and continue to maintain friendly relations. In April 2000, Belarus and Israel signed an agreement on trade, science, culture, and education. The two countries also formed a joint committee to improve relations between the two nations. Belarus has an embassy in Tel Aviv and Israel has an embassy in Minsk.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] The two countries have also discussed implementing a visa-free regime between the two countries. Belarus Foreign Minister [Vladimir Makei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Makei) continues to satisfied with the relations between the two countries and also expressed hope for new opportunities to facilitate further all-round cooperation with Israel in conjunction with fostering progressive development with the Jewish state. Both nations celebrated its 20th anniversary in December 2012. Bilateral cooperation has since then encapsulated tourism, culture, trade, economy, science, education and other fields.[^cite_note-418] In 2013, Belarusian Vice Premier Mikhail Rusyi met top Israeli officials from the Belarusian Agriculture and Food Ministry, the State Committee for Science and Technology, Belarusbank and the Vitebsk Oblast Executive Committee to further facilitate economic cooperation as well as development of innovative technologies into the Belarusian agricultural sector.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421]

### Belgium

Main article: [Belgium–Israel relations](21337666.md)

### Bosnia and Herzegovina

Israel and Bosnia and Herzegovina established diplomatic relations in 1997.[^cite_note-422]

### Bulgaria

Israel and Bulgaria have strong ties. Bulgaria [saved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust_in_Bulgaria) the majority of its Jews during [World War II](32927.md) — 48,000 were rescued, 11,343 were deported.[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424] Israel and Bulgaria established diplomatic relations in 1948. After the Six-Day War Bulgaria cut diplomatic ties with Israel. In 1988, Bulgaria joined the wave of first nations to recognise the [State of Palestine](241405.md), something it has never withdrawn. In 1990 diplomatic relations were renewed. Bulgaria has an embassy in Tel Aviv and Israel has an embassy in [Sofia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia) and honorary consul in [Varna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varna,_Bulgaria).[^cite_note-425]

In January 2012, Israel and Bulgaria signed two memorandum of understanding, one was for joint military training exercises and one for cooperation in the defence sector. The two agreements were signed by Israeli Defense Minister Ehud Barak and Bulgarian Defense Minister [Anyu Angelov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anyu_Angelov). Minister Angelov said the two agreements, in addition to their economic and defense benefits, also "bring a political message – Bulgaria and Israel are a step closer towards stronger cooperation and a strategic dialogue." After the 2012 Burga bus bombing, Bulgaria and Israel pledged to increase security relations between the two countries discussing various aspects of bilateral cooperation at a meeting at the Ministry of Interior between Bulgarian Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Interior Tsvetlin Iochev and Israeli Foreign Affairs Minister, Avigdor Leiberman in November 2013. An international investigation led by Bulgaria has established was the work of people linked with the bombing to the military wing of Hezbollah where five Israeli tourists and a Bulgarian citizen died.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

The governments of both countries also intend to cooperate in the high-tech sector, the communications sector, health care and agriculture and to continue the positive development of tourism between the two countries and energy resource divestment. Israel also intends to join with Bulgaria, Cyprus, Greece and Romania to launch a regional group for crisis response which will boost the partnership between these countries in cases of common security challenges as result of a March 2014 meeting between [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman) and Bulgarian Interior Minister Tsvetlin Yovchev.[^cite_note-428]

### Croatia

Main article: [Croatia–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



Croatian president [Kolinda Grabar-Kitarović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolinda_Grabar-Kitarovi%C4%87) with Israeli president [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin), July 2015

While Croatia was part of the [Yugoslavia Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia) (1943–1991) it established diplomatic relations with Israel in year 1948 through the Federation, but after Israel attacked Yugoslavia's [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) ally [Egypt](8087628.md) in the [Six-Day War](29328.md) in 1967, Yugoslavia severed all diplomatic relations with Israel. After the [breakup of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakup_of_Yugoslavia) occurred as a result of a series of political upheavals and conflicts Croatia declared independence on 8 October 1991 and officially renewed its bilateral relations with Israel which recognized Croatia as an independent state on 16 April 1992, but due to some disagreements with the Croatian President [Tuđman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franjo_Tu%C4%91man) full diplomatic relations were established 5 years later, on 4 September 1997. Since then relations between Croatia and Israel have been excellent. Croatia has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 4 honorary consulates (in [Ashdod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashdod), [Caesarea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarea_\(modern_town\)), [Jerusalem](16043.md) and [Kfar Shmaryahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Shmaryahu)). Israel has an embassy in [Zagreb](55906.md). In addition, Croatia does not recognize the [State of Palestine](241405.md).

### Cyprus

Main article: [Cyprus–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



Chief Rabbi of Cyprus, [Arie Zeev Raskin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arie_Zeev_Raskin), meets the Cypriot President, [Demetris Christofias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demetris_Christofias).

Israel has had diplomatic relations with Cyprus since Israel's independence in 1948, when Cyprus was a British protectorate. Israel and Cyprus' associations have continued to expand since 1960, the year of Cyprus' independence. The neighboring countries trade regularly and there are high flows of tourism between them. However, Cypriot politicians have frequently spoken out against Israeli military raids in the Palestinian territories as well as the 2006 Lebanon War, during which Cyprus was forced to manage a heavy flow of refugees and aid out of and into Lebanon.[^cite_note-429]

However, the discovery of natural gas in the [Leviathan gas field](30257584.md) led to a thawing of relations between the two governments. The two countries demarcated their Exclusive Economic Zones in 2010. Israel's rising antagonism with Turkey further increased the level of cooperation between the two countries, as Israel compensated by improving its relations elsewhere. In February 2012, Netanyahu visited Cyprus, the first visit of an Israeli Prime Minister in history.[^cite_note-430]

Relations between Israel and Cyprus are based on mutual respect for Western values, the support for free-market economy and the establishment of democracies in the Middle East that would co-exist peacefully.[^cite_note-431]

### Czech Republic

Main article: [Czech Republic–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Republic%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Israel and the Czech Republic share a special relationship. Czechoslovakia was the only country to send aid to Israel in its early years e.g. [arms shipments from Czechoslovakia to Israel 1947–1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_shipments_from_Czechoslovakia_to_Israel_1947%E2%80%931949).

In December 2008, the Czech Air Force wanted to train in desert conditions for the upcoming mission in Afghanistan. No country agreed to help, except Israel. Israel saw it as an opportunity to thank the Czechs for training Israeli pilots when the country was first established.[^cite_note-432]

### Denmark

Main article: [Denmark–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

The relations between Denmark and Israel have been friendly and warm. Denmark voted for the [partition of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_Palestine) in 1947 and supports Israel in the [United Nations](31769.md). Denmark was one of few countries in Europe to [save most of its Jewish population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rescue_of_the_Danish_Jews) during the WWII. In [Jerusalem](16043.md), there is a monument to the rescue of [Danish Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danish_Jews), a school is named in Denmark's honor, and the King [Christian X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_X) hospital in [Eitanim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eitanim) is named after Denmark's king during World War II. Denmark has an embassy in Israel, and Israel has an embassy in Copenhagen.

The political lives of the two states have been somewhat intertwined: The former Israeli minister of social and diaspora affairs [Michael Melchior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Melchior) was born in Denmark and is the son of former chief rabbi in Copenhagen, [Bent Melchior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bent_Melchior), the nephew of former Danish minister of traffic and minister of tourism and communication [Arne Melchior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arne_Melchior), and the grandson of the acting rabbi for the Jewish refugees from Denmark in Sweden 1943–45, [Marcus Melchior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Melchior); the executive director of the [Peres Center for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peres_Center_for_Peace) 2001–2011, [Ron Pundak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Pundak) who played an important role in starting the [Oslo peace process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_peace_process) and was part of the core group behind the [Geneva Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Initiative), is the son of the influential Danish journalist [Herbert Pundik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_Pundik); and the prominent Israeli politician [Yohanan Plesner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yohanan_Plesner), former chairman of the [Plesner Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plesner_Committee), is the son of Danish architect [Ulrik Plesner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulrik_Plesner).

### Estonia

Estonia and Israel maintain excellent relations. Israel officially recognized the Republic of Estonia on 4 September 1991 and diplomatic relations were established on 9 January 1992. Foreign Minister Urmas Paet opened the Estonian Embassy in Tel Aviv in November 2009.[^cite_note-433]

As of 2012, bilateral trade with Israel was 19.9 million euros.[^cite_note-434]

### Finland

Main article: [Finland–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finland%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

In 2004, a joint Finland-Israel Technology (FIT) cooperation program was created for research and development projects in the field of ICT. The Office of the Chief Scientist in Israel and [Tekes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tekes_\(agency\)), the Finnish Funding Agency for Research and Innovation, allocated five million euros each for the funding of projects.[^cite_note-agendafin.com-435] In 2005, Finnish exports to Israel totaled 155,24 million euros and imports from Israel to Finland totaled 95.96 million euros. Finland's leading exports to Israel are telecommunications equipment and machinery, and Israeli fruits and vegetables.[^cite_note-agendafin.com-435]

### France

Main article: [France–Israel relations](23970938.md)



[Tzipi Livni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzipi_Livni) and French foreign minister [Douste-Blazy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douste-Blazy)

In the early 1950s, France and Israel maintained close political and military ties as common enemies of Pan-Arab nationalism. France was Israel's main weapons supplier until its withdrawal from Algeria in 1962 removed most common interest from the relationship, and France became increasingly critical of Israel.[^cite_note-436] This new reality became clear when, in the crisis leading up to the [Six-Day War](29328.md) in June 1967, [Charles de Gaulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_de_Gaulle)'s government imposed an arms embargo on the region, mostly affecting Israel, which had relied on France for weapons over the previous decade.[^cite_note-437] Under [François Mitterrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Mitterrand) in the early 1980s, French–Israeli relations improved greatly. Mitterrand was the first French president to visit Israel while in office.[^cite_note-438] In 1967, after the Six-Day War, 5,300 French Jews immigrated to Israel.[^cite_note-439]

### Georgia

Main article: [Georgia–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Relations between Israel and Georgia remain excellent.[^cite_note-Washtimes-440] Georgia's former defense minister from 2006 to 2008, [Davit Kezerashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davit_Kezerashvili), had previously lived in Israel. Israel has been selling weapons to Georgia for seven years financed by grants from the USA[^cite_note-Washtimes-440] Included in these weapons are Israeli-built spy [drones](58900.md) provided through the former mayor of [Tel Aviv](31453.md), [Roni Milo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roni_Milo). Israeli advisors, estimated to number between 100 and 1,000, have trained the Georgian military for some time.[^cite_note-Washtimes-440] The two nations also maintain a visa free policy where Georgian and Israeli officials signed an agreement to lift visa requirements for Georgian citizens traveling to Israel, reciprocating Georgia's visa-free policy for Israelis in place since mid-2005.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442]

Georgia has its embassy in Tel Aviv, and the State of Israel has its embassy in Tbilisi. In June 2013, Georgian Prime Minister [Bidzina Ivanishvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidzina_Ivanishvili) praised the Jewish people and Israel and has sought to increase relations between Israel and Georgia elucidating his special relationship between him, Georgia and the Jewish people. Ivanishvili also wanted to extend strategic partnerships and as well as furthering economic cooperation by making it easier for Israeli entrepreneurs and investors to not just do business in Israel, but also facilitate the right paths to do business for the Georgians. The Israel-Georgia Chamber of Business was established in 1996 in order to facilitate business transactions between the two friendly countries and acting as a guide for Israeli businesses through the Georgian economy. Major sectors include insurance, real estate and construction, medical and dental solutions, industrial and utility projects, and energy.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445]

### Germany

Main article: [Germany–Israel relations](6545438.md)

See also: [East Germany–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Germany%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Due to its role in the Holocaust, Israel was at first extremely hostile towards Germany and initially refused to establish relations with them. However, relations gradually thawed as Germany offered to pay [reparations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reparations_Agreement_between_Israel_and_West_Germany) in 1952, and diplomatic relations were officially established in 1965. Israel and Germany now maintain a "special relationship" based on shared beliefs, Western values and a combination of historical perspectives.[^cite_note-BICOM-446] Among the most important factors in their relations is [Nazi Germany](21212.md)'s role in the [genocide](12441.md) of 6 million Jews during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust).[^cite_note-447]

Germany is a prime supplier of arms to Israel, including [Dolphin submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolphin_class_submarine). The military co-operation has been discreet but mutually profitable: Israeli intelligence, for example, sent captured [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact) armor to West Germany to be analyzed. The results aided the German development of an [anti-tank system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-tank_warfare).[^cite_note-448]

### Greece

Main article: [Greece–Israel relations](16119434.md)

Both Greece and Turkey recognized the State of Israel in the late 1940s, but were diplomatically represented in Tel Aviv on lower-than-embassy levels. Greek–Israeli relations improved in 1995.&#x20;



The Israeli and Greek Navies joined forces in 2012 near [Piraeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piraeus).



Meeting of President of Israel [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) and Prime Minister of Greece [Antonis Samaras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonis_Samaras) in August 2012 in [Athens](1216.md)



[Hellenic Air Force](363254.md) [Bombardier 415s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_415) fighting flames at [Ein Hod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ein_Hod) on 4 December 2010

Greece–Israel relations improved as Turkey–Israel relations worsened in the aftermath of the [2010 Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid). In October 2010, the Israeli and Greek air-forces trained jointly in Greece. According to the BBC, this signified a boost in ties that was due in large part to Israel's rift with Turkey.[^cite_note-449] In November 2011, the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) hosted Greece's [Hellenic Air Force](363254.md) in a joint exercise at the [Uvda base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovda_Airport).[^cite_note-450]

The joint Cyprus–Israel oil and gas explorations are also an important factor for Greece, given its strong links with Cyprus.[^cite_note-451] Additionally Greek and Israeli state controlled energy companies are planning to lay the world's longest subsea power cable, linking Israel, Cyprus and Greece by 2023. The link, called the [EuroAsia Interconnector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroAsia_Interconnector) project, will be the longest in the world.[^cite_note-452]

A new joint action committee for the Greek–Israeli alliance has been created in the U.S. Congress in early 2013. The creation and goals of the Greek-Israeli Caucus under the name Congressional Hellenic-Israel Alliance were announced at a special event held in the Congress.[^cite_note-453] It is co-chaired by Congress members [Gus Bilirakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gus_Bilirakis) the Republican representative from Florida and [Ted Deutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Deutch) the Democrat from Florida, and the Greek-Israeli Caucus consists of powerful members of both Republican and Democratic party. It is estimated that it may become the most important pressure group in Congress by 2014.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456]

### Holy See

Main article: [Holy See–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Before the establishment of the State of Israel in 1948, the [Vatican](13393.md) opposed Zionist policies and objectives in Palestine. In 1947, during discussions at the United Nations about the [United Nations Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine), the Vatican supported the [internationalization of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internationalization_of_Jerusalem), in order to keep the [holy places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_places) away from either Israeli or Arab sovereignty. In October 1948, as the [1948 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War) was in progress, [Pope Pius XII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII), deeply disturbed by that violent conflict, issued the [encyclical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclical) *[In multiplicibus curis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_multiplicibus_curis)*, in which he called on the peace-makers to give Jerusalem and its outskirts "an international character" and to assure – "with international guarantees" – freedom of access and worship at the holy places scattered throughout Palestine. In April 1949, he issued the encyclical *[Redemptoris nostri cruciatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redemptoris_nostri_cruciatus)*, in which he appealed for justice for the [Palestinian refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_refugee) and repeated his call for an "international status" as the best form of protection for the holy places.

In January 1964, Pope [Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_VI) visited Israel, the first such Papal visit.

Following the [Six-Day War](29328.md), the Vatican modified its position on the holy places. In an address to the [College of Cardinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cardinals) in December 1967, [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) called for a "*special statute*, internationally guaranteed" for Jerusalem and the Holy Places, thus changing the previous demand for the internationalization of Jerusalem.

Diplomatic relations between the Israeli government and the Vatican were established in 1994, following the conclusion of the [Fundamental Agreement between the Holy See and the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_Agreement_between_the_Holy_See_and_the_State_of_Israel), signed on 30 December 1993. The Holy See (the Vatican), which has UN observer status, is the only non-UN member state with which Israel has diplomatic relations and the only non-UN member state recognised by Israel. An important organ in these relations is the Israel-Vatican Bilateral Commission, established under article 10 of the Agreement to resolve economic issues between the parties.

In 2000, [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) visited Israel, followed by visits of [Pope Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI) (2009) and [Pope Francis](1687680.md) (2014). The bilateral commission convened on 30 April 2009 and 10 December 2009.[^cite_note-457]

### Hungary

Main article: [Hungary–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Hungary and Israel established full diplomatic relations in 1948 and severed during the Six-Day War in 1967. Relations were restored in 1989 with improved relations as well as the opening of embassies and consulates with Hungary having an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 4 honorary consulates (in [Eilat](66162.md), [Haifa](55606.md), [Jerusalem](16043.md) and [Tel Aviv](31453.md))[^cite_note-458] and Israel has an embassy in [Budapest](36787.md) and an honorary consulate in [Szeged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szeged).[^cite_note-459] Both countries have stressed the increasing of trade and tourism between one another and an estimated 30,000 Hungarian Jews emigrated to Israel in 1948.[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462] Both nations are also members of the [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean).

In January 2022, Israeli Prime Minister [Naftali Bennett](39754840.md) thanked his Hungarian counterpart, Prime Minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md), for the Hungarian government's consistent support of Israel at international forums.[^cite_note-463]

### Ireland

Main article: [Ireland–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Full diplomatic relations between Ireland and Israel were established in 1975.[^cite_note-464] As of 2023, the Israeli ambassador to Ireland is [Dana Erlich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Erlich) and the Irish ambassador to Israel is [Sonya McGuinness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonya_McGuinness).[^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466]

The Irish government followed a similar line to other EU governments during the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), with the Irish [Taoiseach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoiseach), [Bertie Ahern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bertie_Ahern), condemning the actions of Israel as "reckless and disproportionate" and calling for an immediate ceasefire on both sides, while also condemning the actions of Hezbollah.[^cite_note-467] During the conflict, a shipment of bombs that attempted to land in Ireland from USA to Tel Aviv was denied use of Irish airspace and airfields by the Irish Government. The weapons were part of a series of agreed arms shipments between the United States Government and Israel. The shipments were diverted via Scotland, where they also caused controversy.[^cite_note-468]

In 2010, the Israel Defense Forces forcibly boarded an Irish aid ship destined for the Gaza Strip which resulted in worsened relations, Israel's Mossad was also involved in the counterfeiting of Irish passports, and two members of the Israeli ambassador's security staff in Dublin were subsequently deported. In 2010, there were numerous protests at the Israeli embassy in Ireland over the treatment of Palestinians.

In December 2024, Israel announced that it would close its embassy in Dublin due to what it described as "the extreme anti-Israel policies of the Irish government".[^cite_note-469]

### Italy

Main article: [Israel–Italy relations](20570993.md)



The [Israeli](177619.md) and [Italian Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Air_Force) conclude an extensive, two-week joint exercise in [Sardinia](29376.md), 17 November 2010.

Relations between Italy and Israel remain strong, with frequent diplomatic exchanges and a large volume of trade. The Israeli Government has followed with great attention the fight against international terrorism pursued by the Italian Government.

### Kosovo

Main article: [Israel–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations)

On 17 February 2008, Kosovo declared its independence from Serbia, a move Serbia rejects. Kosovo has been [recognized](15808914.md) by a substantial number of UN members, including the United States and most members of the European Union.[^cite_note-470] At the time of the declaration, an Israeli Foreign Ministry official stated: "We haven't decided when we're going to decide, and instead will monitor events and consider the issue." Israel was reluctant to recognize Kosovo's independence, in part because of the possibility of Palestinians using recognition of Kosovo to justify their own unilateral declaration of independence.[^cite_note-471] On 21 September 2018 the president of Kosovo, Hashim Thaci told that he would place the Kosovo embassy in Jerusalem if Israel recognized Kosovo as an independent state.[^cite_note-472] Israel and Kosovo agreed to establish diplomatic relations on 4 September 2020.[^cite_note-473] In March 2022, Kosovo officially opened its embassy in Jerusalem after becoming the first Muslim-majority territory to recognise Jerusalem as Israel's capital.[^cite_note-474]

### Latvia

Latvia and Israel established diplomatic relations 6 January 1992. In October 2012, Foreign Minister [Edgars Rinkēvičs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgars_Rink%C4%93vi%C4%8Ds) called on Israeli officials for the development of economic and trade contacts between both countries and support for Latvia's accession to the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development) (OECD). Rinkevics also met with Israeli President Shimon Peres on behalf of President Berzins to visit Latvia. Rinkevics and Peres agreed on the key significance of education and knowledge-based economies for the development of small countries further paving opportunities for our future cooperation between the two nations.[^cite_note-Baltic_Export-475]

Israeli Foreign Minister Avigdor Lieberman also corroborated claims that Israel is interested in forging closer relations with Latvia as Rinkevics emphasized the 20th anniversary of relations between Latvia and Israel in 2012 and both foreign ministers expressed their commitment to strengthening economic and trade cooperation, including contacts between Latvian and Israeli entrepreneurs and a more cooperative operation in the fields of education, culture and science.[^cite_note-Baltic_Export-475]

### Lithuania

Main article: [Israel–Lithuania relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Lithuania_relations)

Israel recognized Lithuania's independence in 1991. Both countries established diplomatic relation in 1992. Israel has an embassy in [Vilnius](32597.md). Lithuania has an embassy in Tel Aviv and two honorary consulates (in Herzliya and Ramat Gan). Relations are warm and friendly between the two nations and both bi-national trade and tourism has doubled, and two new regularly scheduled flights have just been set between the two countries since 2010.[^cite_note-476]

Lithuanian achievements, particularly Lithuania's achievements in biochemistry and the biosciences have attracted Israeli entrepreneurs and investors to invest in Lithuania's science and technology sector and Lithuania is enthusiastic in combining Israel's knack for unlocked untapped entrepreneurial and intellectual potential combined with Lithuania's strong science and technology research base and talented workforce. Both nations have concluded an agreement on cooperation in industrial research and experimental development as further cooperation within venture capital to fund joint research projects remains underway.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-Dovilė_Jablonskaitė-478]

Bilateral meetings between both nations reached unexpected highs between 2009–2011 and cooperations between the two nations remain excellent with several forms of cooperation that include science, economics, education, and culture as emphasized by Lithuanian Foreign Minister Audronius Azubalis. Lithuanian President Dalia Grybauskaite has stated Israel as model of innovation in which is one of the reasons that Lithuania is interested in enhancing its bilateral relations.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481][^cite_note-482]

In 2011 alone, Israeli tourism to Lithuania grew by 62 percent, and Lithuanian exports to Israel increased by 54 percent. Israel's leading pharmaceutical company, Teva Pharmaceutical Industries Ltd, has a manufacturing plant in Lithuania and TEVA recently invested in "Sicor Biotech", a Lithuanian biotechnological pharmaceutical company, further signifying economic cooperation and encourages the development of such sciences as biotechnology and genetic engineering in Lithuanian universities.[^cite_note-483]

Israeli companies have taken a very active interest in possibilities for carrying out clinical research in Lithuania; many of them are already in cooperation with Lithuanian medical institutions and have invested in Lithuania's burgeoning life sciences sector. In 2013, Lithuanian biotechnology company ProBioSanus signed a representation agreement with Bharat Israel where ProBioSanus' natural, probiotic-based cleaning and personal care products will be available for sale in Israel for the first time. The company already opened stores in Lithuania and Scandinavia. Mr Andrejauskas, head of ProBioSanus spoke of this agreement saying that "We have a strong scientific base and intellectual potential in Lithuania, so we are allocating particular attention to the creation of new formulas and products."[^cite_note-484] Besides Israeli investments in the Lithuanian life science's sector, Lithuanian presence in Israel in the food industry remains strong as Israeli supermarkets sell Lithuanian cheese, curd desserts, Vilnius mayonnaise, Žemaitijos Pienas butter, Panevėžys ice cream, Švyturys-Utenos Alus beers and various styles of Lithuanian liquors. According to Vadimas Ivanovas, a business and financial analyst at Enterprise Lithuania, says that Israeli retail chains are very interested in Lithuanian sweets, pastries, canned vegetables, various sauces, alcoholic beverages, mineral water. "Veal is particularly popular."[^cite_note-Dovilė_Jablonskaitė-478]

### Luxembourg

In November 1947, Luxembourg voted in favor of the partition plan to create a Jewish state. Israel and Luxembourg established full diplomatic relations in 1949. Due to Luxembourg's small size, the Israeli embassy is located in Brussels and Luxembourg is represented politically by the Dutch embassy and economically by the Belgian embassy.[^cite_note-485]

### Moldova

Main article: [Israel–Moldova relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Moldova_relations)



Moldovan Embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md)

Relations between Moldova and Israel were established on 6 June 1992. Israel recognized Moldova on 25 December 1991. Israel is represented in Moldova through its embassy in Chisinau and Moldova has an embassy in Tel Aviv and an honorary consulate in Haifa. Relations between the two countries are friendly and a warm one based on mutual support. A myriad of efforts are primarily focused on the realization of achieved accords on cooperation in the fields of economy, medicine, industry, agriculture, social and cultural issues. Though the volume of trade between Israel and Moldova was low, [Larisa Miculet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larisa_Miculet), the third Ambassador of Moldova to Israel, has stated that there numerous untapped potential of increasing bilateral trade. For Israeli entrepreneurs and investors, Moldova is geopolitically convenient because of its location in the center of Europe, its high transparency between public authorities and foreign investors and due to its having eliminated most of the bureaucratic barriers that hinder business activities. Various business sectors of cooperation between Israel and Moldova range from pharmaceuticals, energy, information technology and software, electronics and electronic equipment, power engineering, metal and plastics processing and construction materials but Moldova has stressed foreign Israeli investments in all segments of the Moldovan economy.[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487][^cite_note-488]

### Netherlands

Main article: [Israel–Netherlands relations](21338002.md)



Netherlands delegation, [Jerusalem March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_March)

In 1947, the Netherlands voted in favor of the establishing Israel and established diplomatic relation in 1949. Israel has an embassy in The Hague and The Netherlands has an embassy in Tel Aviv.

### North Macedonia

Main article: [Israel–North Macedonia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93North_Macedonia_relations)

### Norway

Main article: [Israel–Norway relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Norway_relations)



[With Israel for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/With_Israel_for_Peace) (MIFF), [Oslo](22309.md)

Norway was one of the first countries to recognize Israel on 4 February 1949. Both countries established diplomatic relations later that year. Israel has an embassy which serves Norway and Iceland in Oslo. Norway has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 2 honorary consulates (in [Eilat](66162.md) and [Haifa](55606.md)). [Israels Venner på Stortinget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israels_Venner_p%C3%A5_Stortinget) *(Friends of Israel in the Parliament of Norway)* is a pro-Israel caucus group consisting of members of the Parliament of Norway ([Stortinget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stortinget)).

### Poland

Main article: [Israel–Poland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Poland_relations)

Following the severing of relations after the [Six-Day War](29328.md), Poland was the first [Eastern Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc) country to restore relations with Israel in 1986 (beside Romania, which had continuous relations with Israel). Full diplomatic relations were reestablished in 1990, after the fall of Communism in Poland.

### Portugal

Main article: [Israel-Portugal relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Portugal_relations)

The [Estado Novo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estado_Novo_\(Portugal\)) regime did not recognize Israel. Full diplomatic relations with the Portuguese government were established on 12 May 1977, following the [Portuguese revolution of 1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnation_Revolution).[^cite_note-489]

### Romania

Main article: [Israel–Romania relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Romania_relations)

Romania and Israel established full diplomatic relations on 11 June 1948. Between 1967 and 1989, Romania was the only Eastern Bloc country to maintain relations with Israel, after the Six-Day War.[^cite_note-490]

Israel has an embassy in [Bucharest](36877.md).[^cite_note-491] Romania has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 3 honorary consulates (in [Haifa](55606.md), [Jerusalem](16043.md) and [Tel Aviv](31453.md)).[^cite_note-492] The two countries have signed many bilateral treaties and agreements and both of them are full members of the [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean) and many other international organizations.

### Russia and the Soviet Union

Main article: [Israel–Russia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Russia_relations)

See also: [Soviet Union and the Arab–Israeli conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union_and_the_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_conflict) and [Russia and the Arab–Israeli conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia_and_the_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_conflict)



[Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) at the [2018 Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade)

The [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) voted in favor of the [United Nations Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) in 1947, which paved the way for the creation of the State of Israel. On 17 May 1948, on the third day of Israel's independence, the [Soviet Union granted *de jure* recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union_and_the_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_conflict#Establishment_of_the_State_of_Israel) to the State of Israel, becoming only the second country to recognise Israel (preceded only by the United States' *de facto* recognition) and the first country to grant Israel *de jure* recognition.[^cite_note-493]

The Soviet Union and the other [communist states of Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc) (with the exception of Romania) cut diplomatic ties with Israel during the Six-Day War. Relations were restored on 19 October 1991, a few months before the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union), despite the fact that hostile Arab countries such as Syria also maintain close ties with Russia. Russia is known to supply Syria with weapons.[^cite_note-494]

In September 2010, Israel and Russia signed a comprehensive military agreement that will "increase cooperation on combating terrorism" and the proliferation of nuclear weapons. Israeli Defense Minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) met with his Russian counterpart, [Anatoly Serdyukov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatoly_Serdyukov) and Prime Minister [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), and signed the agreement during a ceremony in Moscow. The Russian military plans on purchasing additional Israeli unmanned aerial vehicles. Russia previously bought 12 drones from the [Israel Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Aerospace_Industries) following the [war in South Ossetia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_South_Ossetia_war).[^cite_note-495]

### Serbia

Main article: [Israel–Serbia relations](20081805.md)

Diplomatic relations between Israel and Serbia's predecessor state, [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia), were severed for twenty-four years, from 1967 until 1991, when they were officially renewed, by which time Yugoslavia was in the process of disintegration. Diplomatic relations continued when, in April 1992, the two remaining Yugoslav republics, Serbia and Montenegro, formed the [Federal Republic of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro) (renamed to [Serbia and Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro) in February 2003). On 3 June 2006, Montenegro and Serbia dissolved the union.

### Slovakia

Both countries established diplomatic relations in 1993. Israel has an embassy in [Bratislava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislava), with its first [resident ambassador in Slovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Israel_to_Slovakia) being [Yael Rubinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yael_Rubinstein).[^cite_note-496] Slovakia has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).[^cite_note-497] In May 2008, Slovak Prime Minister [Robert Fico](4127226.md) called [Ehud Olmert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Olmert) and [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) for the strengthening of diplomatic relations between Israel and Slovakia. An Israel – Slovakia Chamber of Commerce and Industry also exists to facilitate further economic and business cooperation between the two countries.[^cite_note-498][^cite_note-499]

### Slovenia

Main article: [Israel–Slovenia relations](27538681.md)

Israel and Slovenia established full diplomatic relations when Israel officially recognized Slovenia on 16 April 1992 and on 28 April 1992 when the countries signed the Protocol establishing diplomatic relations between each other. The Slovenian-Israeli Chamber of Commerce was established in 2010 signifying a strengthening of ties and both countries have discussed bilateral cooperation in business, tourism, science and technology, and agriculture.[^cite_note-500][^cite_note-501]

#### Slovenian Jews in Israel

A sizable Slovenian Jewish community was presently in the [Gush Dan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Dan) area.

### Spain

Main article: [Israel–Spain relations](21337570.md)

[Francoist Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francoist_Spain) did not recognize Israel, citing an [international conspiracy of Jews and Freemasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Masonic_conspiracy_theory) against Spain. Israel and Spain have maintained diplomatic ties since 1986. Nevertheless, Israeli exports to Spain are on the rise, totalling \$870 million in 2006, and Israeli firms doing business with Spain include the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea) Works, [Haifa](55606.md) Chemicals, [Amdocs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amdocs), [Comverse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comverse) and [Teva Pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teva_Pharmaceuticals).[^cite_note-502] The Spanish foreign minister visited Israel for an official visit in May 2008.[^cite_note-503]

Since the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), Spain has been among the [most outspoken critics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_reactions_to_the_Gaza_war) about Israel's actions. As of September 2025, both countries have recalled their respective ambassadors.[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505]

### Sweden

Main article: [Israel–Sweden relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Sweden_relations)

Sweden voted in favor of the [United Nations Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) in 1947. Relations between Sweden and Israel were good during the 1950s and 1960s, and Sweden expressed strong support for Israel during the [Six-Day War](29328.md).[^cite_note-506] However, from 1969, Sweden became more critical of Israel.[^cite_note-ahlmark1997_196-507][^cite_note-508] In October 2014, Sweden became the first [European Union](9317.md) member to officially recognize the [State of Palestine](241405.md).[^cite_note-509]

### Switzerland

Main article: [Israel–Switzerland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Switzerland_relations)



The Chief of the [Swiss Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Switzerland), André Blattmann, visiting Israel as a guest of [IDF](39237.md) [Chief of General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_General_Staff_\(Israel\)), [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz), 27 November 2011

The [First Zionist Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Zionist_Congress) was held in Basel in 1897, and 15 out of a total of 22 congresses were held in Switzerland. Before the establishment of the State of Israel, Switzerland maintained a consulate in Jerusalem and a consular agency in Tel Aviv. It recognized the new state in 1949 and opened a consulate in Tel Aviv, which was upgraded to an embassy in 1958. The Swiss community in Israel is the largest in the Asian region, totalling around 12,000 persons.[^cite_note-switzerland1-510]

After escalation of the Middle East conflict, Switzerland halted arms sales and military cooperation with Israel from 2002 to 2005. Since 2004, there has been regular political dialogue between Switzerland and Israel.[^cite_note-switzerland1-510]

Switzerland has represented Israel's interests in numerous countries (Hungary (1967–1989), Guinea (1967–1973), Ceylon/Sri Lanka (1970–1976), Madagascar (1973–1994), Liberia (1973–1983) and Ghana (1973–2002)). Conversely, it has represented the interests of Iran (1958–1987) and Ivory Coast (1973–1986) in Israel. It also lobbied successfully for inclusion of Magen David Adom in the Red Cross and Red Crescent movement.[^cite_note-switzerland1-510]

On 21 April 2009, Israel recalled its ambassador for consultations due to events that occurred at the UN anti-racism conference in Geneva, Switzerland. Israeli officials, angered by a meeting between Swiss President [Hans-Rudolf Merz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans-Rudolf_Merz) and the Iranian president, recalled its ambassador to Switzerland, Ilan Elgar "for consultations" amid ongoing controversy over an anti-racism conference being held in Geneva.[^cite_note-511][^cite_note-512]

### Ukraine

Main article: [Israel–Ukraine relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations)

Both countries established diplomatic relations on 26 December 1991. Israel has an embassy in [Kyiv](585629.md). Ukraine has an embassy in Tel Aviv and a consulate-general in Haifa. In July 2010 the foreign ministers of two countries sign an agreement of non-visa traffic between Israel and Ukraine. This came into effect on 9 February 2011 and since then Ukrainians and Israelis may enter territory, travel through it or stay on Ukraine/Israel without having to obtain visas for 90 days within a period of 180 days.

### United Kingdom

Main article: [Israel–United Kingdom relations](20968669.md)

Israel established [diplomatic relations with the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_United_Kingdom) in 1950.[^cite_note-britain-513]

- Israel maintains an [embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_London) in London.

  [^cite_note-britain-513]

- The United Kingdom is accredited to Israel through its [embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_Kingdom,_Tel_Aviv) in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).

  [^cite_note-514]

The UK governed [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine) from 1921 until 1948, when it achieved full independence. Both countries share common membership of the [OECD](33853117.md), and the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization). Bilaterally the two countries have a [Trade and Partnership Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_the_United_Kingdom),[^cite_note-515] a Reciprocal Healthcare Agreement,[^cite_note-516] and a Strategic Partnership.[^cite_note-517] The two countries are currently negotiating a new [Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_Free_Trade_Agreement).[^cite_note-518]

Relations between the Israel and the UK began as hostile. During the [1948 Arab–Israeli war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_war), Britain detained 8,000 Jewish men of military age attempting to make [aliyah](351513.md) to Israel in Cyprus, so they could not participate in the fighting. Britain supplied weapons to the Arab states, and almost went to war with Israel. When Israel captured the Negev, the British Ministry of Defence began to draw up plans for a possible invasion of Israel. British planes spied on Israeli positions, and war between the two countries became even more possible when four British planes were shot down by Israel. However, the two countries began to soften later on, and trade began. In 1956 Israel invaded [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) in agreement with France and the UK that [invaded Suez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Crisis). Nevertheless, Anglo-Israeli relations became turbulent in the summer of 2006 when Prime Minister [Tony Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blair), along with many other [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Europe) leaders criticized [IDF](39237.md) airstrikes against [Hezbollah](13919.md) targets in Lebanon, which had high civilian casualties. During the [Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) premiership, relations between the two countries continued to remain close.

## Central America

### Belize

- Both countries established diplomatic relations in 1983.

  [^cite_note-519]

- Belize is represented in Israel through its embassy in London.

  [^cite_note-520]

- Israel is represented in Belize through its embassy in [San Salvador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Salvador).

  [^cite_note-521]

- Since 2011, both countries have an agreement on visa exemption for holders of diplomatic, service and national passports.

  [^cite_note-522]

- In November 2023, Belize suspended diplomatic ties with Israel over its refusal to implement a ceasefire for the [Gaza war](74998519.md).

  [^cite_note-523]

### Costa Rica

Main article: [Israel-Costa Rica relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Costa_Rica_relations)

Costa Rica was one of the first countries to vote in favor of the establishment of the State of Israel. It maintained its embassy in Jerusalem until August 2006, when it finally relocated to Tel Aviv in order to bolster its ties with the Arab world.[^cite_note-524] In December 2011, Rodrigo Carreras became the Costa Rican ambassador to Israel for the second time, after his posting there in the 1980s. Carreras' father, Benjamin Nunez, also served as the Costa Rican ambassador to Israel.[^cite_note-525]

### Guatemala

Main article: [Israel-Guatemala relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Guatemala_relations)

Following Israel's independence, Guatemala was the first country to open an embassy in Jerusalem in 1959. The embassy was later relocated to Tel Aviv. However, on 16 May 2018 Guatemala again moved its embassy back to Jerusalem. The move to Jerusalem followed two days after the opening of the [United States' Embassy to Israel](57417950.md) in the city.[^cite_note-526]

### Haiti



Israeli rescue workers in Haiti

Haiti and Israel maintain full diplomatic relations. In 1947, Haiti voted for the [UN Partition Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Partition_Plan) and the creation of the State of Israel.[^cite_note-527] Israel was among the first to send both personnel and aid to Haiti following the [devastating earthquake in 2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake).[^cite_note-528]

### Dominican Republic

- Both countries established diplomatic relations in 1979.

- Israel is represented in Dominican Republic through its embassy in [Santo Domingo](57638.md), [Dominican Republic](8060.md).

- Both countries have an agreement on visa abolition.

  [^cite_note-529]

### Panama

Main article: [Israel-Panama relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Panama_relations)

Panama formally recognized the State of Israel on 18 June 1948. The two countries first exchanged in 1960.

Panama voted with Israel against UN resolution 67/19,[^cite_note-530] as well as UN resolution 70/70,[^cite_note-531] and in 2012 had a 30% voting coincidence with Israel.[^cite_note-532]

### Nicaragua

1974–1978: Israel sold arms to [Somoza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anastasio_Somoza_Debayle) *régime*.[^cite_note-533][^cite_note-534]

On 1 June 2010, Nicaragua suspended diplomatic ties with Israel in response to the [Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid).[^cite_note-NicaraguaSuspend-535] Relations were restored in March 2017.[^cite_note-toi_nicaragua-536]

In October 2024, Nicaragua once again severed diplomatic ties with Israel due to the [Israeli war on Gaza](74998519.md) and [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon), calling the Israeli government "fascist" and "genocidal".[^cite_note-ilcut-24]

## North America

### Canada

Main article: [Canada–Israel relations](16077496.md)

Canada's relationship with Israel began in 1947, when Canada was represented on the United Nations Special Committee on Palestine (UNSCOP). Canada was one of the 33 countries (including only four [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) members) that voted in favour of the [1947 UN partition Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine), thus beginning a longstanding relationship with the Jewish state based on a shared commitment to democratic values, understanding, and mutual respect.

### Cuba

Main article: [Cuba–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



Cuban ambassador to Israel with [Golda Meir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golda_Meir), 1960

Cuba sent troops to fight against Israel during the [War of Attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Attrition). Cuba also joined the expeditionary forces during the 1973 [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War), and broke diplomatic relations with Israel the same year.

As Cuba's enemy and a very close [United States ally](3449549.md), Israel was the only country in the world that consistently sided with the U.S. blockade against Cuba.

In late 2010, Fidel Castro, who at this time no longer held office in Cuba's government, stated that he believes Israel has a 'right to exist', which is a shift from his *régime'*&#x73; earlier policy.[^cite_note-537]

### Mexico

Main article: [Israel–Mexico relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Mexico_relations)



Israeli Prime Minister [Benyamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benyamin_Netanyahu) meeting with Mexican President [Enrique Peña Nieto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Pe%C3%B1a_Nieto) in Jerusalem, 2016

Mexico and Israel have had diplomatic relations since January 1950. Throughout the years, they have maintained close relations with each other. In 2000, a free trade agreement was signed between the two nations. Mexico has also purchased weapons from Israel and is one of Israel's closest allies in the Americas.[^cite_note-538]

### United States

Main article: [Israel–United States relations](3449549.md)

See also: [Israel–United States military relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations)



[Yitzhak Shamir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Shamir) with U.S. Secretary of Defense [Caspar Weinberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caspar_Weinberger), 1982

The relations between Israel and the United States have evolved from an initial United States policy of sympathy and support for the creation of a Jewish state in 1948 (It was the first country to recognize the establishment of the State) to an unusual partnership that links Israel with the United States trying to balance competing interests in the Middle East region. The United States has been considered Israel's most powerful and supportive ally and hosts the annual [Salute to Israel Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salute_to_Israel_Parade) in New York City. From 1948 to 2012, the United States has provided Israel with \$233.7 billion in aid (after adjusting for inflation). In addition, the US has provided Israel with \$19 billion in loan guarantees.[^cite_note-539]

The United States is Israel's largest trading partner, accounting for 22.4 percent of Israel's \$43.19 billion in imports, and 42.1 percent of Israel's \$40.14 billion in exports annually (2005).[^cite_note-USDoS-540] The U.S. also provides Israel with \$2.4 billion in military assistance annually, which is equivalent to 24.5 percent of Israel's military expenditures. (2005).[^cite_note-USDoS-540]

## Oceania

### Australia

Main article: [Australia–Israel relations](27679535.md)



[Australian Light Horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Light_Horse) Monument in [Australian Soldier Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Soldier_Park), [Beersheba](312755.md)

Australia was one of the four [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) nations to vote in favour of the [1947 UN partition resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947_UN_partition_resolution). Australia and Israel established full diplomatic relations in January 1949.[^cite_note-541] Australia has an embassy in Tel Aviv and Israel likewise in [Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canberra). In May 2010, the Australian government expelled an Israeli diplomat over the misuse of Australian passports in the [assassination of Mahmoud Al-Mabhouh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Mahmoud_Al-Mabhouh), which Foreign Minister Stephen Smith said was "not the act of a friend."[^cite_note-542]

### Marshall Islands

Main article: [Israel–Marshall Islands relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Marshall_Islands_relations)

The Marshall Islands is one of the most consistent supporters of Israel international affairs, along with the United States, Micronesia and Palau. The Marshall Islands is a country in free association with the United States, and thus consistently vote in favor of the United States.

### Micronesia

Main article: [Israel–Federated States of Micronesia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Federated_States_of_Micronesia_relations)

The Federated States of Micronesia is one of the most consistent supporters of Israel in voting at the UN.[^cite_note-543][^cite_note-544]

In January 2010, the President of the Federated States of Micronesia, [Emanuel Mori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emanuel_Mori), and the President of the Republic of Nauru, [Marcus Stephen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Stephen), with their foreign ministers, visited Israel to expand ties on issues such as [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel#Health_sciences), [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Israel), [water conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel#Hydraulic_engineering), [clean technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel#Environmental_science) and other areas in which Israel can provide expertise. They met with Israeli leaders including the Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu, President Shimon Peres and Minister of Foreign Affairs Avigdor Lieberman.[^cite_note-545]

### Nauru

Main article: [Israel–Nauru relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Nauru_relations)

Israel and the [Republic of Nauru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Nauru) established diplomatic relations in December 1994.[^cite_note-Nauru-162] Nauru recognized Jerusalem as Israel's capital in 2019.[^cite_note-546]

### New Zealand

Main article: [Israel–New Zealand relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations)

New Zealand was one of the four [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) nations to vote in favour of the [1947 UN partition resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947_UN_partition_resolution). Since then, most New Zealand governments have been supportive of Israel. After 53 years of full diplomatic relations, the Israeli Embassy in Wellington closed in 2004 due to \$5.4 million in cost-cuts by the Israeli Foreign Ministry. It was speculated that trade with Arab countries were a major factor. In June 2004, the New Zealand Government criticized Israel's policy of bulldozing Palestinian homes and donated \$534,000 to aid homeless Palestinians. At one time there were four missions in the South Pacific area in Canberra, Sydney, Wellington and Suva in Fiji. Following the closure, only Canberra remained open, which was responsible for New Zealand-Israeli Relations. In 2009, the Israel Foreign Ministry announced the reopening of the embassy in [Wellington](33804.md).[^cite_note-JP2009-403]

In mid-2004, two suspected [Mossad](27619007.md) agents were jailed for three months and paid a \$35,000 fine for trying on false grounds to obtain a New Zealand passport. High-level visits between the two countries were cancelled, visa restrictions imposed for Israeli officials, and an expected visit to New Zealand by Israeli president Moshe Katsav was cancelled. More than a year later, Israel apologized and New Zealand Prime Minister Helen Clark announced that it was time to resume friendly diplomatic relations with Israel.

### Niue

Diplomatic relations between Israel and [Niue](21228.md) were established on 3 August 2023.[^cite_note-547]

### Palau

Main article: [Israel–Palau relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Palau_relations)

Palau is one of the most consistent supporters of Israel in the United Nations and other international affairs along with the United States, Micronesia and the Marshall Islands. In 2006, Palau had the highest voting coincidence with Israel in the United Nations.

### Papua New Guinea

Main article: [Israel–Papua New Guinea relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Papua_New_Guinea_relations)

### Tuvalu

Israel maintains a non-resident embassy to Tuvalu in Jerusalem and Tuvalu has voted together with Israel against UN resolutions furthering Palestinian sovereignty on occasion.[^cite_note-548][^cite_note-549]

## South America

### Argentina

Main article: [Argentina–Israel relations](30057872.md)



[Golda Meir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golda_Meir) with [Eva Perón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eva_Per%C3%B3n)

In 1992, three Israeli diplomats were killed in the bombing of the Israeli Embassy which left 29 people dead and 240 wounded. Two years later, another bombing took place at the [AMIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asociaci%C3%B3n_Mutual_Israelita_Argentina) Jewish community center in Buenos Aires, killing 85 people and wounding 300. The investigation was never completed.[^cite_note-550] [Néstor Kirchner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%A9stor_Kirchner) called this a national disgrace, and reopened the files.

### Bolivia

Main article: [Bolivia–Israel relations](58066729.md)

In January 2009, Bolivia limited its foreign relationship with Israel in the wake of strikes in [Gaza](12047.md) by Israel. Bolivian President [Evo Morales](284163.md) has reportedly promised to take Israel to an international court for alleged war crimes committed in Gaza.[^cite_note-551] Bolivia originally granted visa free access to Israeli citizens. However, as a result of Israel's 2014 military operation in Gaza, which it opposed, President Evo Morales declared Israel a "terrorist state" and his government will now require Israeli citizens to obtain a visa to visit Bolivia.[^cite_note-552] Morales has called Israel's treatment of Palestinians "a genocide".[^cite_note-553]

Following [Morales's resignation](62307909.md) and fleeing to Mexico in November 2019, relations were restored to their previous status.[^cite_note-554]

On 1 November 2023, following the escalation of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), Deputy Foreign Minister of Bolivia [Freddy Mamani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddy_Mamani) announced that Bolivia would be cutting diplomatic ties with Israel. This made Bolivia the first country to cut relations with Israel in response to the conflict.[^cite_note-555]

Bolivia and Israel officially reestablished relations on 9 December 2025, following [Rodrigo Paz Pereira](69731047.md)'s assumption of office.[^cite_note-556] Israelis were granted visa-free entry to Bolivia.[^cite_note-557]

### Brazil

Main article: [Brazil–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil%E2%80%93Israel_relations)



[Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) and Foreign Minister of Brazil, [Celso Amorim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celso_Amorim)

Brazil played a large role in the [establishment of the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Establishment_of_the_State_of_Israel). Brazil held the Presidency office of the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) in 1947, which proclaimed the [Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine). The Brazilian delegation to the U.N., supported and heavily lobbied for the partition of Palestine toward the creation of the [State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Israel). Brazil was also one of the first countries to recognize the State of Israel, on 7 February 1949, less than one year after [Israeli Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence).

Nowadays, Brazil and Israel maintain close political, economic and military ties. Brazil is a full member state of [Israel Allies Caucus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Allies_Caucus),[^cite_note-558] a political advocacy organization that mobilizes pro-Israel parliamentarians in governments worldwide. The two nations enjoy a degree of arms cooperation as Brazil is a key buyer of Israeli weapons and military technology.[^cite_note-559] Also, Brazil is Israel's largest trading partner in Latin America.[^cite_note-560] Israel has an embassy in [Brasília](4752.md) and a consulate-general in [São Paulo](390875.md) and Brazil has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and an honorary consulate in [Haifa](55606.md).[^cite_note-561] A longstanding dispute between Israel and Brazil is ongoing over Brazil's official rejection of a new Israeli ambassador because of his ties to the West Bank.[^cite_note-562]

Brazil-Israel relations improved significantly during the presidency of [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) (2019 - 2023). Brazilian president Bolsonaro expressed his love for Israel several times.[^cite_note-563] He even said to have turned Brazil into Israel's new best friend.[^cite_note-564] In December 2019, Brazil opened a trade office in Jerusalem. Brazil also considered to move its embassy to Israel from Tel Aviv to Jerusalem.[^cite_note-565]

Brazil has the ninth largest [Jewish community in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_population_by_country), about 107,329 by 2010, according to the [IBGE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBGE) census.[^cite_note-566] The Jewish Confederation of Brazil (CONIB) estimates to more than 120,000.[^cite_note-567]

### Chile

Main article: [Chile–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile%E2%80%93Israel_relations)

Chile recognized Israel's independence in February 1949. Israel sent its first ambassador to Chile in May 1950, and Chile sent its first ambassador to Israel in June 1952. Prime Minister [Golda Meir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golda_Meir) visited Chile during her term in office. In March 2005, the Chilean minister of foreign affairs Ignacio Walker made an official visit to Israel. Chile is home to the [largest Palestinian community outside the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinians_in_Chile), approximately 500,000.[^cite_note-568][^cite_note-569]

### Colombia

Main article: [Colombia–Israel relations](15647808.md)



President [Iván Duque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Duque) with President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md)

Colombia and Israel established formal relations in the mid-1950s. In recent years, Colombia has purchased planes, drones, weapons and intelligence systems from Israel. An Israeli company, Global CST won a \$10 million contract in Colombia.[^cite_note-570] On 1 May 2024, Colombian president Petro announced Colombia would break diplomatic ties with Israel.[^cite_note-571] In August 2026, Colombia’s new president, [Abelardo de la Espriella](81665680.md), announced that he will resume diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-572] On 10 August 2026, Colombia officially recognized Israeli sovereignty over the [Golan Heights](7515865.md).[^cite_note-573]

### Guyana

Both countries have established diplomatic relations on 9 March 1992.[^cite_note-574]

### Paraguay

In September 2018, Paraguay announced that it was moving its embassy in Israel out of Jerusalem and back to Tel Aviv, just months after a previous Paraguayan administration had opened the new mission. The US, Guatemala and Paraguay, all moved their embassies to Jerusalem in May 2018. Paraguay said it had officially recognized the military wings of Palestinian group [Hamas](13913.md) and Lebanon's [Hezbollah](13919.md) as terrorist organizations, drawing praise from Israel. President [Mario Abdo Benitez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Abdo_Ben%C3%ADtez) made the declaration in an official document in August 2019.[^cite_note-575]

### Peru

Main article: [Israel–Peru relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Peru_relations)

Israel and Peru established diplomatic relations in 1957. In 1998 the two countries began talks on a free-trade agreement.[^cite_note-trade-576] Israel sent rescue teams and medical aid to Peru after earthquakes in 1970, 2005 and 2007.[^cite_note-Israeli_mission_to_aid_Peru-577][^cite_note-578] In 2001, [Eliane Karp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliane_Karp), a former Israeli, became the First Lady of Peru.[^cite_note-579]

### Suriname

[Suriname](26828.md) and Israel established diplomatic relations on 24 February 1976.[^cite_note-580] In February 2011, non-resident Israeli ambassador to Suriname, Amiram Magid, criticized the government of Suriname for recognizing the State of Palestine.[^cite_note-581] On 30 May 2022, in a meeting with Israeli Foreign Minister [Yair Lapid](2448072.md), Surinamese Foreign Minister [Albert Ramdin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Ramdin) announced plans to open a Surinamese embassy in Jerusalem in the near future.[^cite_note-582] On 23 June, Surinamese President [Chan Santokhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chan_Santokhi) announced the cancellation of the Jerusalem embassy plans, citing a lack of funds.[^cite_note-583]

### Uruguay

Main article: [Israel–Uruguay relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Uruguay_relations)

Israel has an embassy in [Montevideo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montevideo). Uruguay has an embassy in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and 2 honorary consulates in [Ashdod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashdod) and [Haifa](55606.md). Uruguay was one of the first nations to recognise Israel as independent.

### Venezuela

Main article: [Israel–Venezuela relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Venezuela_relations)

Relations were once strong, but the bilateral ties soured under the [Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Venezuela) of [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) in Venezuela.[^cite_note-584] The Jewish population in Venezuela, which peaked at 45,000,[^cite_note-Hurricane-585] is now below 15,000 "as a result of severe instability in the country", according to the Israeli [Stephen Roth Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Roth_Institute).[^cite_note-SRI-586] *[The Miami Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Miami_Herald)*, *Jewish Times*, and Jewish organizations have reported large-scale emigration of Jewish people from Venezuela during the Chávez administration.[^cite_note-587][^cite_note-588]

Israel and Venezuela resumed consular relations on 11 August 2026, after 17 years.[^cite_note-589]

## Israeli foreign aid

Main article: [Israeli foreign aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_foreign_aid)

Israeli foreign aid comprises primarily [development assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Development_assistance) and [humanitarian aid](434679.md) provided by Israel to foreign countries. Israel provides assistance to developing countries to alleviate and solve economic and social problems through its international cooperation program of technical assistance, based on its own recent and ongoing experience in developing human and material resources. Israel's Agency for International Development Cooperation, established as an agency of the [Israeli Foreign Ministry](18133296.md) in 1958 and known by its Hebrew acronym, *MASHAV*, is the primary vehicle for providing this aid.[^cite_note-Benad-590]

This center trains course participants from approximately 140 countries on healthcare, as well as emergency and disaster medicine, and has participated in dozens of projects worldwide in fields economic fields such as agriculture, education, development, employment, and healthcare, as well as humanitarian fields such as disaster relief, reconstruction, and refugee absorption.[^cite_note-591]
